## A Bibliographical Note / 版本说明
===Original===

Barry Lyndon﻿—far from the best known, but by some critics acclaimed as the finest, of Thackeray’s works﻿—appeared originally as a serial a few years before *Vanity Fair* was written; yet it was not published in book form, and then not by itself, until after the publication of *Vanity Fair*, *Pendennis*, *Esmond* and *The Newcomes* had placed its author in the forefront of the literary men of the day. So many years after the event we cannot help wondering why the story was not earlier put in book form; for in its delineation of the character of an adventurer it is as great as *Vanity Fair*, while for the local colour of history, if I may put it so, it is no undistinguished precursor of *Esmond*.
In the number of *Fraser’s Magazine* for January 1844 appeared the first instalment of “*The Luck of Barry Lyndon, Esq., a Romance of the Last Century*, by FitzBoodle,” and the story continued to appear month by month﻿—with the exception of October﻿—up to the end of the year, when the concluding portion was signed “G. S. FitzBoodle.” “Fitzboodle’s Confessions,” it should be added, had appeared occasionally in the magazine during the years immediately precedent, so that the pseudonym was familiar to *Fraser’s* readers. The story was written, according to its author’s own words, “with a great deal of dullness, unwillingness and labour,” and was evidently done as the instalments were required, for in August he wrote “read for ‘B. L.’ all the morning at the club,” and four days later of “ ‘B. L.’ lying like a nightmare on my mind.” The journey to the East﻿—which was to give us in literary results *Notes of a Journey from Cornhill to Grand Cairo*﻿—was begun with *Barry Lyndon* yet unfinished, for at Malta the author noted on the first three days of November﻿—“Wrote Barry but slowly and with great difficulty.” “Wrote Barry with no more success than yesterday.” “Finished Barry after great throes late at night.” In the number of Fraser’s for the following month, as I have said, the conclusion appeared. A dozen years later, in 1856, the story formed the first part of the third volume of Thackeray’s *Miscellanies*, when it was called *Memoirs of Barry Lyndon, Esq., Written by Himself*.

===Chinese===

《巴里·林登》（Barry Lyndon）——远不是萨克雷（Thackeray）作品中名气最大的，却被一些批评家推为其最佳之作——最初以连载形式问世，比《名利场》（Vanity Fair）动笔还早几年；然而直到《名利场》《潘登尼斯》《埃斯蒙德》和《纽克姆一家》相继出版、把作者送上当世文坛最前列之后，它才得以成书印行，而且不是单独刊行。事隔这么多年，我们不免要问：这部小说为何迟迟未能成书？须知单论刻画一个冒险家的性格，它不逊于《名利场》；而论历史的乡土色彩——恕我这样措辞——它也是《埃斯蒙德》一位不容小觑的先行者。
1844年1月号的《弗雷泽杂志》（Fraser's Magazine）刊出了第一部分：《巴里·林登绅士的运气，上个世纪的一段传奇》，菲茨布德尔（FitzBoodle）著。此后除十月外，故事逐月连载，直至年底；末一部的署名是“G. S. 菲茨布德尔”。应当补充一句：此前几年，《菲茨布德尔的自白》已在该刊时断时续地发表，所以这个笔名对《弗雷泽》的读者并不陌生。
用作者自己的话说，这部小说写得“极其沉闷、极不情愿、极其吃力”；而且显然是连载催一截写一截：八月里他写道“整个上午在俱乐部为《巴·林》查资料”，四天后又写“‘巴·林’像一场噩梦压在我心头”。
那趟东方之旅——后来结出的文学成果是《从康希尔到开罗的旅行札记》——启程时《巴里·林登》尚未完稿：十一月的头三天，作者在马耳他记道——“写巴里，进展缓慢，万分艰难。”“写巴里，不见起色，一如昨日。”“深夜，几经阵痛，巴里完稿。”如前所述，翌月号的《弗雷泽》上，结局便登了出来。
十二年后，即1856年，这篇小说作为萨克雷《杂文集》第三卷的第一部分出版，题为《巴里·林登绅士自述回忆录》。

===Original===

Since then, it has nearly always been issued with other matter, as though it were not strong enough to stand alone, or as though the importance of a work was mainly to be gauged by the number of pages to be crowded into one cover. The scheme of the present edition fortunately allows fitting honour to be done to the memoirs of the great adventurer.
To come from the story as a whole to the personality of the eponymous hero. Three widely-differing historical individuals are suggested as having contributed to the composite portrait. Best known of these was that very prince among adventurers, G. J. Casanova de Seingalt, a man who in the latter half of the eighteenth century played the part of adventurer﻿—and generally that of the successful adventurer﻿—in most of the European capitals; who within the first five-and-twenty years of his life had been “abbé, secretary to Cardinal Aquaviva, ensign, and violinist, at Rome, Constantinople, Corfu, and his own birthplace (Venice), where he cured a senator of apoplexy.” His autobiography, *Mémoires Écrit Par Lui-Même* (in twelve volumes), has been described as “unmatched as a self-revelation of scoundrelism.” It has also been suggested, with I think far less colour of probability, that the original of Barry was the diplomatist and satiric poet Sir Charles Hanbury Williams, whom Dr. Johnson described as “our lively and elegant though too licentious lyrick bard.” The third original, and one who, there cannot be the slightest doubt, contributed features to the great portrait, is a certain Andrew Robinson Stoney, afterwards Stoney-Bowes.
The original of the Countess Lyndon was Mary Eleanor Bowes, Dowager Countess of Strathmore, and heiress of a very wealthy Durham family. This lady had many suitors, but in 1777 Stoney, a bankrupt lieutenant on half pay, who had fought a duel on her behalf, induced her to marry him, and subsequently hyphenated her name with his own. He became member of Parliament, and ran such extravagant courses as does Barry Lyndon, treated his wife with similar barbarity, abducted her when she had escaped from him, and then, after being divorced, found his way to a debtors’ prison. There are similarities here which no seeker after originals can overlook. Mrs. Ritchie says that her father had a friend at Paris, “a Mr.

自那以后，它几乎总是同其他作品合刊发行，仿佛它单薄得不能独立成书，又仿佛一部作品的重要性，主要得看一个封皮下塞进了多少页码。所幸本版的编排，让这位大冒险家的回忆录得到了应得的体面。
从小说整体转到主人公其人。一般认为，有三位于史可考、迥然不同的人物，共同贡献了这幅合成画像。其中最著名的，当属冒险家中的亲王——G. J. 卡萨诺瓦·德·塞恩加尔特（Casanova de Seingalt）：十八世纪下半叶，他在欧洲大多数京城扮演冒险家的角色——而且多半是得手的冒险家；他一生头二十五年里，先后“在罗马、君士坦丁堡、科孚和他的出生地威尼斯当过修道院士、阿夸维瓦红衣主教的秘书、旗手和小提琴手，还在威尼斯治好过一位参议员的中风”。他的自传《亲笔回忆录》（Mémoires Écrit Par Lui-Même，十二卷），有人形容为“恶棍的自我揭露，无出其右”。也有人提出，巴里的原型是外交家兼讽刺诗人查尔斯·汉伯里·威廉斯爵士（Sir Charles Hanbury Williams）——约翰逊博士形容他是“我们那位轻快优雅、只是太放纵的抒情歌手”——不过依我看，这一说成色的可能性要小得多。
第三位原型，则毫无疑问为这幅大画像贡献了诸多面相，那就是一个叫安德鲁·鲁滨逊·斯托尼（Andrew Robinson Stoney）的人，后来改姓斯托尼-鲍斯（Stoney-Bowes）。
林登伯爵夫人的原型，是玛丽·埃莉诺·鲍斯（Mary Eleanor Bowes），斯特拉斯莫尔太夫人，达勒姆一户豪门的继承人。这位女士的追求者众多，但1777年，斯托尼——一个领半薪的破产中尉，曾为她决斗过一场——说动她嫁给了他，随后又在自己的姓氏上连上了她的姓。他当上了议员，穷奢极欲一如巴里·林登；虐待妻子同样野蛮；妻子逃出他的魔掌，他又将她劫回；最后离了婚，进了债务人监狱。这些相似之处，凡是追寻原型的人，都不可能视而不见。里奇太太（Mrs. Ritchie）说，她父亲在巴黎有一位朋友，“一位鲍斯先生（Mr.）

===Original===

Bowes, who may have first told him this history of which the details are almost incredible, as quoted from the papers of the time.” The name of Thackeray’s friend is a curious coincidence, unless, as may well have been the case, he was a connection of the family into which the notorious adventurer had married. It is not unlikely that Thackeray had seen the work published in 1810﻿—the year of Stoney-Bowes’s death﻿—in which the whole unhappy romance was set forth. This was “*The Lives of Andrew Robinson Bowes Esq., and the Countess of Strathmore*. Written from thirty-three years’ Professional Attendance, from letters and other well authenticated Documents by Jesse Foot, Surgeon.” In this book we find several incidents similar to ones in the story. Bowes cut down all the timber on his wife’s estate, but “the neighbours would not buy it.” Such practical jokes as Barry Lyndon played upon his son’s tutor were played by Bowes on his chaplain. The story of Stoney and his marriage will be found briefly given in the notice of the Countess’s life in the *Dictionary of National Biography*.
Whence that part of the romantic interlude dealing with the stay in the Duchy of X﻿—﻿—, dealt with in chapter X, etc., was inspired, Thackeray’s own notebooks (as quoted by Mrs. Ritchie) conclusively show: “January 4, 1844. Read in a silly book called *L’Empire*, a good story about the first K. of Wurtemberg’s wife; killed by her husband for adultery. Frederic William, born in 1734 (?), m. in 1780 the Princess Caroline of Brunswick Wolfenbüttel, who died the 27th September 1788. For the rest of the story see *L’Empire, Ou Dix Ans Sous Napoléon, Par Un Chambellan*: Paris, Allardin, 1836; vol. I 220.” The “Captain Freny” to whom Barry owed his adventures on his journey to Dublin (chapter III) was a notorious highwayman, on whose doings Thackeray had enlarged in the fifteenth chapter of his *Irish Sketch Book*.
Despite the slowness with which it was written, and the seeming neglect with which it was permitted to remain unreprinted, *Barry Lyndon* was to be hailed by competent critics as one of Thackeray’s finest performances, though the author himself seems to have had no strong regard for the story. His daughter has recorded, “My father once said to me when I was a girl: ‘You needn’t read *Barry Lyndon*, you won’t like it.’

===Chinese===

——说不定最早把这段往事讲给他听的正是此人；细节几乎令人难以置信，俱引自当时的报纸。”
萨克雷的这位朋友姓鲍斯，这是个耐人寻味的巧合——除非，这很可能，他本就是那位臭名昭著的冒险家所娶人家的亲戚。萨克雷未必没有读过1810年——正是斯托尼-鲍斯去世那年——出版的那部书，整段不幸罗曼史在其中和盘托出。书名是《安德鲁·鲁滨逊·鲍斯绅士与斯特拉斯莫尔伯爵夫人传：据三十三年随侍亲见及信件与其他确凿文件写成，外科医生杰西·富特（Jesse Foot）著》。此书里有好几桩事迹同小说情节相仿。鲍斯把妻子田产上的树木砍伐一空，可“邻人都不肯买”。巴里·林登对他儿子家庭教师的那些恶作剧，鲍斯原样搬到他的私人牧师头上。斯托尼其人及其婚姻，在《国家传记词典》伯爵夫人条目里有简略记述。
至于小说第十章等处写到的那段留居X——公国的浪漫插曲灵感何来，萨克雷自己的笔记本（据里奇太太引用）给了确凿答案：“1844年1月4日。在一本叫《帝国》的蠢书里读到个好故事，讲符腾堡第一代国王的王后；因奸情被丈夫杀死。腓特烈·威廉，生于1734年（？），1780年娶不伦瑞克-沃尔芬比特尔的卡罗琳公主，公主卒于1788年9月27日。其余情节见《帝国，或侍从官笔下的拿破仑治下十年》：巴黎，阿拉尔丹，1836年；卷一第220页。”
巴里赴都柏林（Dublin）途中的历险所系的那位“弗雷尼上尉”（第三章），是个臭名昭著的绿林大盗，萨克雷在《爱尔兰素描集》第十五章里曾对其行迹大书特书。
尽管写得迟缓，尽管多年不见重印、貌似备受冷落，《巴里·林登》日后仍被有识的批评家奉为萨克雷最精彩的作品之一，虽然作者本人似乎对它并无多深的眷爱。他的女儿记道：“我小时候，父亲有一次对我说：‘《巴里·林登》你不必读，你不会喜欢的。’

===Original===

Indeed, it is scarcely a book to *like*, but one to admire and to wonder at for its consummate power and mastery.” Another novelist, Anthony Trollope, has said of it: “In imagination, language, construction, and general literary capacity, Thackeray never did anything more remarkable than *Barry Lyndon*.” Mr. Leslie Stephen says: “All later critics have recognised in this book one of his most powerful performances. In directness and vigour he never surpassed it.”
W. J.

===Chinese===

“的确，这几乎不是一本让人*喜欢*的书，而是一本让人叹服、让人惊奇于其炉火纯青的功力与驾驭的书。”另一位小说家安东尼·特罗洛普（Anthony Trollope）这样评价它：“论想象力、语言、结构和全面的文学才力，萨克雷再没有写出过比《巴里·林登》更出色的东西。”莱斯利·斯蒂芬先生（Mr. Leslie Stephen）则说：“所有后来的批评家都公认，这本书是他最有力的作品之一。论直截了当与遒劲有力，他从未超出此书。”
W. J.

## III A False Start in the Genteel World / 三 上流社会里的一步错棋
===Original===

I rode that night as far as Carlow, where I lay at the best inn; and being asked what was my name by the landlord of the house, gave it as Mr. Redmond, according to my cousin’s instructions, and said I was of the Redmonds of Waterford county, and was on my road to Trinity College, Dublin, to be educated there. Seeing my handsome appearance, silver-hiked sword, and well-filled valise, my landlord made free to send up a jug of claret without my asking; and charged, you may be sure, pretty handsomely for it in the bill. No gentleman in those good old days went to bed without a good share of liquor to set him sleeping, and on this my first day’s entrance into the world, I made a point to act the fine gentleman completely; and, I assure you, succeeded in my part to admiration. The excitement of the events of the day, the quitting my home, the meeting with Captain Quin, were enough to set my brains in a whirl, without the claret; which served to finish me completely. I did not dream of the death of Quin, as some milksops, perhaps, would have done; indeed, I have never had any of that foolish remorse consequent upon any of my affairs of honour: always considering, from the first, that where a gentleman risks his own life in manly combat, he is a fool to be ashamed because he wins. I slept at Carlow as sound as man could sleep; drank a tankard of small beer and a toast to my breakfast; and exchanged the first of my gold pieces to settle the bill, not forgetting to pay all the servants liberally, and as a gentleman should. I began so the first day of my life, and so have continued. No man has been at greater straits than I, and has borne more pinching poverty and hardship; but nobody can say of me that, if I had a guinea, I was not freehanded with it, and did not spend it as well as a lord could do.
I had no doubts of the future: thinking that a man of my person, parts, and courage, could make his way anywhere. Besides, I had twenty gold guineas in my pocket; a sum which (although I was mistaken) I calculated would last me for four months at least, during which time something would be done towards the making of my fortune. So I rode on, singing to myself, or chatting with the passersby; and all the girls along the road said God save me for a clever gentleman!

===Chinese===

当晚我骑到卡洛，住进那里最好的客栈；店主人问起我的名姓，我按表哥的嘱咐自称雷德蒙（Redmond）先生，说自己是沃特福德郡雷德蒙家的人，正要上都柏林的三一学院去念书。店主见我仪表堂堂、银柄佩剑、行囊饱满，主动送上一壶红葡萄酒，不消吩咐；这笔账他算进单子里的价钱，您可以料想，相当可观。在那美好的旧日，没有一位绅士不灌下足量的酒就上床的；而在我踏入世界的头一天，我决意把阔绅士做足全套；我向您担保，这一角色我演得令人叹服。白天种种事件的激动——离家出走、会晤奎因上尉——早已够我脑袋发昏，红葡萄酒只是把我彻底送了终。我没有梦见奎因的死，换了某些奶油小生，说不定会梦见；说实在的，凡我历次荣誉之事，我从不曾有过那种愚蠢的悔恨：我从头一天起就认定，一位绅士既在堂堂之阵上拿性命相搏，赢了倒害臊，那才是傻子。那一夜我在卡洛睡得人事不省；早晨一罐淡啤酒加一片吐司；掬出头一块金币付账，赏钱给得慷慨，绅士本色，一个子儿不含糊。我人生的头一天这样开头，此后也一贯如此。谁也没有比我更窘迫过，谁也没有比我受过更紧的穷、吃过的苦更多；可是谁也不能说我有了几尼不是大手大脚，不会像位爵爷一样把钱花个漂亮。
我对前途毫无疑虑：想着凭我这样的人才、头脑和胆量，走到哪儿都闯得开。何况我口袋里还有二十金几尼；这笔钱（虽然我算错了）我盘算着至少够撔四个月，四个月里，我发迹的事业总该有个眉目。于是我一路骑一路唱，同过路人搭话；沿路的姑娘们都求上帝保佑我这样一位俊俏的绅士！

===Original===

As for Nora and Castle Brady, between today and yesterday there seemed to be a gap as of half-a-score of years. I vowed I would never reenter the place but as a great man; and I kept my vow too, as you shall hear in due time.
There was much more liveliness and bustle on the king’s high road in those times, than in these days of stagecoaches, which carry you from one end of the kingdom to another in a few score hours. The gentry rode their own horses or drove in their own coaches, and spent three days on a journey which now occupies ten hours; so that there was no lack of company for a person travelling towards Dublin. I made part of the journey from Carlow towards Naas with a well-armed gentleman from Kilkenny, dressed in green and a gold cord, with a patch on his eye, and riding a powerful mare. He asked me the question of the day, and whither I was bound, and whether my mother was not afraid on account of the highwaymen to let one so young as myself to travel? But I said, pulling out one of them from a holster, that I had a pair of good pistols that had already done execution, and were ready to do it again; and here, a pockmarked man coming up, he put spurs into his bay mare and left me. She was a much more powerful animal than mine; and, besides, I did not wish to fatigue my horse, wishing to enter Dublin that night, and in reputable condition.
As I rode towards Kilcullen, I saw a crowd of the peasant-people assembled round a one-horse chair, and my friend in green, as I thought, making off half a mile up the hill. A footman was howling “Stop thief!” at the top of his voice; but the country fellows were only laughing at his distress, and making all sorts of jokes at the adventure which had just befallen.
“Sure you might have kept him off with your blunder*bush*!” says one fellow.
“Oh, the coward! to let the Captain *bate* you; and he only one eye!” cries another.
“The next time my Lady travels, she’d better lave you at home!” said a third.
“What is this noise, fellows?” said I, riding up amongst them, and, seeing a lady in the carriage very pale and frightened, gave a slash of my whip, and bade the red-shanked ruffians keep off. “What has happened, madam, to annoy your Ladyship?” I said, pulling off my hat, and bringing my mare up in a prance to the chair window.
The lady explained.

===Chinese===

至于诺拉和布雷迪城堡，今天同昨天之间隔着仿佛十年的鸿沟。我发誓，不混出个人样绝不再进那个门；这誓我也守住了——您到时自会听到。
那些年月，王家大道上的热闹忙碌，远非如今这些驿站马车可比——如今的车，几十个钟头就把您从王国这头拉到那头。绅士们骑自家的马、乘自家的车，三天走完如今十小时的路程；所以去都柏林的旅人一路从不缺伴。从卡洛往纳斯去的一程，我同一位基尔肯尼来的先生结伴：他武装齐全，一身绿衣配金绦，一只眼罩着黑布，骑一匹高头母马。他同我攀谈时下的日堂话题，问我上哪儿去，还问我母亲放我这样年纪的人上路，不怕路上的强盗么？我立刻从枪套里拔出一支手枪给他看，说这对家伙已经开过杀戒，随时再开一回；正说着，一个麻脸汉子骑近前来，他便一夹马腹，撈下我走了。他那匹马比我的强得多；再说我也惜我的马力——我要当晚赶进都柏林，还得像个人样。
骑到基尔卡伦附近，我看见一群乡下人围着一辆单马轿车，我那位绿衣朋友——我当是他——正往半英里外的山头逃。一个男仆扯着嗓子嚎“抓贼！”；乡巴佬们却只拿他的狼狈取乐，为方才落到他头上的祸事说着各色风凉话。
“你不是有杆大喷子么，怎么不挡他一挡！”一个说。
“呸，脓包！让队长揍你——他还独眼哩！”另一个嚷。
“下回太太出门，顶好把你撂在家里！”第三个说。
“嚷嚷什么，伙计们？”我策马挤进人群，见车中一位夫人面色惨白、惊魂未定，扬手一鞭，喝令那些红腿的粗坯退开。“出了什么事，夫人，惊扰了您？”我摘下帽子，让我的母马打着响鼻碎步凑到车窗前。
夫人讲明了原委。

===Original===

She was the wife of Captain Fitzsimons, and was hastening to join the Captain at Dublin. Her chair had been stopped by a highwayman: the great oaf of a servant-man had fallen down on his knees armed as he was; and though there were thirty people in the next field working when the ruffian attacked her, not one of them would help her; but, on the contrary, wished the Captain, as they called the highwayman, good luck.
“Sure he’s the friend of the poor,” said one fellow, “and good luck to him!”
“Was it any business of ours?” asked another. And another told, grinning, that it was the famous Captain Freny, who, having bribed the jury to acquit him two days back at Kilkenny assizes, had mounted his horse at the gaol door, and the very next day had robbed two barristers who were going the circuit.
I told this pack of rascals to be off to their work, or they should taste of my thong, and proceeded, as well as I could, to comfort Mrs. Fitzsimons under her misfortunes. “Had she lost much?” “Everything: her purse, containing upwards of a hundred guineas; her jewels, snuffboxes, watches, and a pair of diamond shoe-buckles of the Captain’s.” These mishaps I sincerely commiserated; and knowing her by her accent to be an Englishwoman, deplored the difference that existed between the two countries, and said that in *our* country (meaning England) such atrocities were unknown.
“You, too, are an Englishman?” said she, with rather a tone of surprise. On which I said I was proud to be such: as, in fact, I was; and I never knew a true Tory gentleman of Ireland who did not wish he could say as much.
I rode by Mrs. Fitzsimon’s chair all the way to Naas; and, as she had been robbed of her purse, asked permission to lend her a couple of pieces to pay her expenses at the inn: which sum she was graciously pleased to accept, and was, at the same time, kind enough to invite me to share her dinner. To the lady’s questions regarding my birth and parentage, I replied that I was a young gentleman of large fortune (this was not true; but what is the use of crying bad fish? my dear mother instructed me early in this sort of prudence) and good family in the county of Waterford; that I was going to Dublin for my studies, and that my mother allowed me five hundred per annum. Mrs. Fitzsimons was equally communicative.

===Chinese===

她是菲茨西蒙斯（Fitzsimons）上尉的太太，正赶去都柏林同上尉团聚。她的车被一个路劫强人拦下；那个蠢牛似的男仆，全副武装，扑通跪倒；强人动手时，隔壁地里明明有三十个人在做工，没有一个肯帮她，反倒祝那位“队长”——他们管那强人叫队长——好运。
“他是穷人的朋友，”一个说，“祝他好运！”
“关我们什么事？”另一个问。还有一个咧着嘴讲：那就是大名鼎鼎的弗雷尼队长，两天前在基尔肯尼巡回审判上买通陪审团脱了罪，从监狱门口翻身上马，第二天就劫了两位出巡回庭的律师。
我喝令这帮无赖滚回去做工，不然尝尝我的鞭子；随后尽力安慰遭了难的菲茨西蒙斯太太。“损失得多吗？”“全光了：钱袋，里头一百多几尼；首饰、鼻烟盒、怀表，还有上尉的一副钻石鞋扣。”这些不幸我诚恳地哀悼了一番；听口音她是英格兰人，我便痛陈两国间的差异，说在我们国家（意指英格兰）这种暴行闻所未闻。
“您也是英格兰人？”她说，语气颇有点惊讶。我答，我以此为荣；事实上我也确实以此为荣；爱尔兰真正的托利绅士，我还没见过一个不恨不得自己也能这么说的。
我一路伴着菲茨西蒙斯太太的车到纳斯；她钱袋被劫，我便请求借她两枚金币付客栈开销：这笔数目她雅量俯纳，同时好意邀我共进晚餐。关于我的出身门第，我答夫人问：我是沃特福德郡一位家产豪富的少年绅士（这不是实话；可臭鱼喊破了又有何用？这一手谨慎，我亲爱的母亲从小便教我），门第清贵；此去都柏林求学，母亲每年给我五百镑。菲茨西蒙斯太太也不吝交底。

===Original===

She was the daughter of General Granby Somerset of Worcestershire, of whom, of course, I had heard (and though I had not, of course I was too well-bred to say so); and had made, as she must confess, a runaway match with Ensign Fitzgerald Fitzsimons. Had I been in Donegal?﻿—No! That was a pity. The Captain’s father possesses a hundred thousand acres there, and Fitzsimonsburgh Castle’s the finest mansion in Ireland. Captain Fitzsimons is the eldest son; and, though he has quarrelled with his father, must inherit the vast property. She went on to tell me about the balls at Dublin, the banquets at the Castle, the horse-races at the Phoenix, the ridottos and routs, until I became quite eager to join in those pleasures; and I only felt grieved to think that my position would render secrecy necessary, and prevent me from being presented at the Court, of which the Fitzsimonses were the most elegant ornaments. How different was her lively rattle to that of the vulgar wenches at the Kilwangan assemblies! In every sentence she mentioned a lord or a person of quality. She evidently spoke French and Italian, of the former of which languages I have said I knew a few words; and, as for her English accent, why, perhaps I was no judge of that, for, to say the truth, she was the first *real* English person I had ever met. She recommended me, further, to be very cautious with regard to the company I should meet at Dublin, where rogues and adventurers of all countries abounded; and my delight and gratitude to her may be imagined, when, as our conversation grew more intimate (as we sat over our dessert), she kindly offered to accommodate me with lodgings in her own house, where her Fitzsimons, she said, would welcome with delight her gallant young preserver.
“Indeed, madam,” said I, “I have preserved nothing for you.” Which was perfectly true; for had I not come up too late after the robbery to prevent the highwayman from carrying off her money and pearls?
“And sure, ma’am, them wasn’t much,” said Sullivan, the blundering servant, who had been so frightened at Freny’s approach, and was waiting on us at dinner. “Didn’t he return you the thirteenpence in copper, and the watch, saying it was only pinchbeck?”

===Chinese===

她是伍斯特郡格兰比·萨默塞特将军的女儿——这位将军我当然听说过（就算没听说，我当然也教养良好，绝不会露出来）；而且她承认，是同旗官菲茨杰拉德·菲茨西蒙斯私奔结合的。我去过多尼戈尔吗？——没有！可惜了。上尉的父亲在那里坐拥十万英亩，菲茨西蒙斯堡是爱尔兰第一等的宅邸。菲茨西蒙斯上尉是长子；虽然同父亲闹翻了，那万贯家财终究归他。她接着给我讲都柏林的舞会、城堡的宴会、凤凰公园的赛马、化装舞会和沙龙，讲得我心痒难耐，恨不得立刻投身这些乐事；我只愁自己的处境须得隐姓埋名，进不得宫廷——菲茨西蒙斯夫妇正是那宫廷上最风雅的点缀。她那一口伶俐的谈吐，同基尔万根舞会上那些俗丫头是何等天壤！她句句不离一位勋爵或贵人。她分明会讲法语和意大利语——前者我说过我懂几个词；至于她的英国口音，呃，我也许没资格评判，因为说句实话，她是我平生见到的第一位真正的英国人。她还进一步忠告我，到了都柏林要千万留心交往的人，那里各国流氓冒险家多得很；待到谈兴渐浓（我们坐在餐后甜点前），她好心提出，让我到她家里寄住——她说，她的菲茨西蒙斯定会喜出望外地欢迎她这位英勇的少年救命恩人。
“说实在的，夫人，”我说，“我可没救下您什么。”这话千真万确；抢劫过后我才赶到，拦不住强人捲走她的钱和珍珠，不是么？
“可不是嘛，太太，也没多少东西，”那个冒失男仆沙利文说——弗雷尼露面时吓破胆的就是他，此刻正侍候我们用饭。“他不是退给你十三便士铜板和那只表了么，还说了句那不过是假货。”

===Original===

But his lady rebuked him for a saucy varlet, and turned him out of the room at once, saying to me when he had gone, “that the fool didn’t know what was the meaning of a hundred-pound bill, which was in the pocketbook that Freny took from her.”
Perhaps had I been a little older in the world’s experience, I should have begun to see that Madam Fitzsimons was not the person of fashion she pretended to be; but, as it was, I took all her stories for truth, and, when the landlord brought the bill for dinner, paid it with the air of a lord. Indeed, she made no motion to produce the two pieces I had lent to her; and so we rode on slowly towards Dublin, into which city we made our entrance at nightfall. The rattle and splendour of the coaches, the flare of the linkboys, the number and magnificence of the houses, struck me with the greatest wonder; though I was careful to disguise this feeling, according to my dear mother’s directions, who told me that it was the mark of a man of fashion never to wonder at anything, and never to admit that any house, equipage, or company he saw, was more splendid or genteel than what he had been accustomed to at home.
We stopped, at length, at a house of rather mean appearance, and were let into a passage by no means so clean as that at Barryville, where there was a great smell of supper and punch. A stout red-faced man, without a periwig, and in rather a tattered nightgown and cap, made his appearance from the parlour, and embraced his lady (for it was Captain Fitzsimons) with a great deal of cordiality. Indeed, when he saw that a stranger accompanied her, he embraced her more rapturously than ever. In introducing me, she persisted in saying that I was her preserver, and complimented my gallantry as much as if I had killed Freny, instead of coming up when the robbery was over. The Captain said he knew the Redmonds of Waterford intimately well: which assertion alarmed me, as I knew nothing of the family to which I was stated to belong. But I posed him, by asking *which* of the Redmonds he knew, for I had never heard his name in our family. He said he knew the Redmonds of Redmondstown. “Oh,” says I, “mine are the Redmonds of Castle Redmond;” and so I put him off the scent. I went to see my nag put up at a livery-stable hard by, with the Captain’s horse and chair, and returned to my entertainer.

===Chinese===

可他家太太骂他放肆奴才，立刻把他撵出屋去，人一走就对我说：“那蠢才不懂一张一百镑的票子是什么——就在弗雷尼抢走的记事本里。”
倘若我在世事上再老成些，本该开始看出菲茨西蒙斯太太并非她自命的那路时髦人物；可我当时把她的故事照单全收；饭馆送账单来，我以爵爷的气派付了。确实，我借她的两枚金币，她毫无掣出来之意；我们就这样慢慢悠悠往都柏林去，入夜进了城。马车的铮铮与烜赫、火把童子的光焰、房屋的众多与壮丽，叫我惊叹不已；不过我照亲爱的母亲的教导，小心把这份惊叹掩住——她告诉我，时髦人物的标记，就是对什么都不露惊奇，对所见的一切房舍、车马、排场，绝不承认比自家的更堂皇、更体面。
最后我们停在一家外表相当寒仁的宅子前，让进一条过道，那过道比起巴里维尔的家远谈不上干净，满屋子晚饭和潘趣酒的浓味。一个红脸膛的壮实汉子从客厅出来，没戴假发，披一件相当破旧的睡袍，戴一顶便帽，同他太太（来者正是菲茨西蒙斯上尉）热烈拥抱。待看见有生人同行，他拥抱得越发如火如荼。太太引见我时，一口咬定我是她的救命恩人，把我的英勇夸了个够，倒像我宰了弗雷尼，而不是劫案收了场才赶到。上尉说，沃特福德的雷德蒙家他熟得很：这话吓了我一跳，因为我对我冒名的那家亲戚一无所知。可我反将他一军，问他认得哪一房的雷德蒙——我们家里可从没听过他的名字。他说他认得雷德蒙斯敦的雷德蒙家。“哦，”我说，“我家是城堡雷德蒙的雷德蒙。”这一下把他甩出了嗅迹。我跟着去近旁一家代役马厩，看着我的马同上尉的马车一道安顿好，回到东道主家。

===Original===

Although there were the relics of some mutton-chops and onions on a cracked dish before him, the Captain said, “My love, I wish I had known of your coming, for Bob Moriarty and I just finished the most delicious venison pasty, which his Grace the Lord Lieutenant sent us, with a flask of Sillery from his own cellar. You know the wine, my dear? But as bygones are bygones, and no help for them, what say ye to a fine lobster and a bottle of as good claret as any in Ireland? Betty, clear these things from the table, and make the mistress and our young friend welcome to our home.”
Not having small change, Mr. Fitzsimons asked me to lend him a tenpenny-piece to purchase the dish of lobsters; but his lady, handing out one of the guineas I had given her, bade the girl get the change for that, and procure the supper; which she did presently, bringing back only a very few shillings out of the guinea to her mistress, saying that the fishmonger had kept the remainder for an old account. “And the more great big blundering fool you, for giving the gold piece to him,” roared Mr. Fitzsimons. I forget how many hundred guineas he said he had paid the fellow during the year.
Our supper was seasoned, if not by any great elegance, at least by a plentiful store of anecdotes, concerning the highest personages of the city; with whom, according to himself, the Captain lived on terms of the utmost intimacy. Not to be behindhand with him, I spoke of my own estates and property as if I was as rich as a duke. I told all the stories of the nobility I had ever heard from my mother, and some that, perhaps, I had invented; and ought to have been aware that my host was an impostor himself, as he did not find out my own blunders and misstatements. But youth is ever too confident. It was some time before I knew that I had made no very desirable acquaintance in Captain Fitzsimons and his lady; and, indeed, went to bed congratulating myself upon my wonderful good luck in having, at the outset of my adventures, fallen in with so distinguished a couple.
The appearance of the chamber I occupied might, indeed, have led me to imagine that the heir of Fitzsimonsburgh Castle, county Donegal, was not as yet reconciled with his wealthy parents; and, had I been an English lad, probably my suspicion and distrust would have been aroused instantly.

===Chinese===

上尉面前虽只盛着残剩的几块羊排和洋葱，搁在一只裂了纹的盘子里，他却说：“亲爱的，早知道你来就好了。鲍勃·莫里亚蒂和我刚吃完一只顶鲜美的鹿肉馅饼，是总督大人派人送来的，还附了他自家酒窖里一瓶西勒里酒。你知道那酒吧，亲爱的？不过既往不可追，追也无益——一只大龙虾，一瓶爱尔兰头一份的红葡萄酒，你说怎么样？贝蒂，把这些东西撤了，让太太和我们的少年朋友宾至如归。”
菲茨西蒙斯先生身上没有零钱，问我借一枚十便士银币去买龙虾；他家太太却掬出我给的一枚几尼，叫女佣去兑开采买晚饭；女佣很快回来，只带回区区几个先令，说鱼贩子把余下的扣了充旧账。“那你就更是个天大的糊涂蛋，把金子交给他！”菲茨西蒙斯先生咆哮。他一年里付给那家伙多少几百几尼，我忘了他说的是几百。
这顿晚饭的佐料，纵无多大雅致，至少有一大堆段子，全关乎本城最高层的人物；照上尉自己的说法，他同他们个个过从极密。我也不肯让他独占，谈起自家的田产家业，口气俨然富可敌国。我把从母亲那里听来的贵族轶事讲了个遍，有几个或许还是我现编的；我本该由此警觉东道主本人就是个骗子——他竟没有拆穿我的漏洞和胡诌。可青年人永远过分自信。过了好一阵我才知道，菲茨西蒙斯上尉夫妇这样一对，绝非可意的相识；那晚上床时，我竟还庆幸自己鸿运当头：闯荡伊始便结交了如此显赫的一对。

===Original===

But perhaps, as the reader knows, we are not so particular in Ireland on the score of neatness as people are in this precise country; hence the disorder of my bedchamber did not strike me so much. For were not all the windows broken and stuffed with rags even at Castle Brady, my uncle’s superb mansion? Was there ever a lock to the doors there, or if a lock, a handle to the lock or a hasp to fasten it to? So, though my bedroom boasted of these inconveniences, and a few more; though my counterpane was evidently a greased brocade dress of Mrs. Fitzsimons’s, and my cracked toilet-glass not much bigger than a half-crown, yet I was used to this sort of ways in Irish houses, and still thought myself in that of a man of fashion. There was no lock to the drawers, which, when they *did* open, were full of my hostess’s rouge-pots, shoes, stays, and rags; so I allowed my wardrobe to remain in my valise, but set out my silver dressing-apparatus upon the ragged cloth on the drawers, where it shone to great advantage.
When Sullivan appeared in the morning, I asked him about my mare, which he informed me was doing well. I then bade him bring me hot shaving-water, in a loud dignified tone.
“Hot shaving-water!” says he, bursting out laughing (and I confess not without reason). “Is it yourself you’re going to shave?” said he. “And maybe when I bring you up the water I’ll bring you up the cat too, and you can shave her.” I flung a boot at the scoundrel’s head in reply to this impertinence, and was soon with my friends in the parlour for breakfast. There was a hearty welcome, and the same cloth that had been used the night before: as I recognised by the black mark of the Irish-stew dish, and the stain left by a pot of porter at supper.
My host greeted me with great cordiality; Mrs. Fitzsimons said I was an elegant figure for the Phoenix; and indeed, without vanity, I may say of myself that there were worse-looking fellows in Dublin than I. I had not the powerful chest and muscular proportion which I have since attained (to be exchanged, alas! for gouty legs and chalk-stones in my fingers; but ’tis the way of mortality), but I had arrived at near my present growth of six feet, and with my hair in buckle, a handsome lace jabot and wristbands to my shirt, and a red plush waistcoat, barred with gold, looked the gentleman I was born.

===Chinese===

不过，我那间卧房的模样，本该让我想到：多尼戈尔郡菲茨西蒙斯堡的继承人，同他豪富的双亲尚未和好；换了个英国少年，疑心和戒备怕是立刻就起来了。可读者诸君知道，我们爱尔兰人在整洁上不像这个一丝不苟的国度的人那样讲究；所以卧房的零乱于我并不扎眼。我舅舅布雷迪那座华厦，窗子不也全破了、塞着破布么？那里几时有过门锁？就算有锁，锁上有把儿么？有搭扣扣得上么？所以，我的卧房纵有这诸多不便，外加几样别的——我的床罩分明是菲茨西蒙斯太太一件油亮了的锦缎裙子，我那面裂了纹的梳妆镜还没有半克朗银币大——我对爱尔兰人家这一套早已惯了，照旧当自己是置身时髦人家。抽屉没有锁，勉强拉开时，塞满女房东的胭脂盒、鞋、束胸和破布；我便让衣裳留在行囊里，只把银质梳妆器具摆上抽屉上那条破布——摆在那里，光可鉴人，大出风头。
早晨沙利文进来，我问起我的母马，他说安好。我随即以洪亮尊严的嗓音吩咐他打剃须热水来。
“剃须热水！”他放声大笑（我承认不为无理）。“您要给您自个儿剃须？等我把热水给您端上来，我把猫也给您端上来，您连它一块儿剃了。”我一只靴子砸在这混蛋头上，回敬他的无礼；随后很快同朋友们在客厅用早餐。欢迎是热烈的，桌布还是头晚那张：我可以认出来——爱尔兰炖锅的黑印子还在，晚餐那罐黑啤的渍子也还在。
东道主热诚迎我；菲茨西蒙斯太太说我这副身段去凤凰公园再体面没有；说句不自夸的话，都柏林比我难看的伙计也确有其人。我那时还没有后来练成的那副胸膛和筋骨（唉，如今换了痛风腿和手指上的痛风石！可人终归要老的），可我已长到如今的六英尺上下；头发卷着，衬衫缀着漂亮的花边胸褶和袖口，穿一件金条纹的红长毛绒马甲，一副天生绅士的气派。

===Original===

I wore my drab coat with plate buttons, that was grown too small for me, and quite agreed with Captain Fitzsimons that I must pay a visit to his tailor, in order to procure myself a coat more fitting my size.
“I needn’t ask whether you had a comfortable bed,” said he. “Young Fred Pimpleton (Lord Pimpleton’s second son) slept in it for seven months, during which he did me the honour to stay with me, and if *he* was satisfied, I don’t know who else wouldn’t be.”
After breakfast we walked out to see the town, and Mr. Fitzsimons introduced me to several of his acquaintances whom we met, as his particular young friend Mr. Redmond, of Waterford county; he also presented me at his hatter’s and tailor’s as a gentleman of great expectations and large property; and although I told the latter that I should not pay him ready cash for more than one coat, which fitted me to a nicety, yet he insisted upon making me several, which I did not care to refuse. The Captain, also, who certainly wanted such a renewal of raiment, told the tailor to send him home a handsome military frock, which he selected.
Then we went home to Mrs. Fitzsimons, who drove out in her chair to the Phoenix Park, where a review was, and where numbers of the young gentry were round about her; to all of whom she presented me as her preserver of the day before. Indeed, such was her complimentary account of me, that before half-an-hour I had got to be considered as a young gentleman of the highest family in the land, related to all the principal nobility, a cousin of Captain Fitzsimons, and heir to £10,000 a year. Fitzsimons said he had ridden over every inch of my estate; and ’faith, as he chose to tell these stories for me, I let him have his way﻿—indeed, was not a little pleased (as youth is) to be made much of, and to pass for a great personage. I had little notion then that I had got among a set of impostors﻿—that Captain Fitzsimons was only an adventurer, and his lady a person of no credit; but such are the dangers to which youth is perpetually subject, and hence let young men take warning by me.
I purposely hurry over the description of my life in which the incidents were painful, of no great interest except to my unlucky self, and of which my companions were certainly not of a kind befitting my quality.

===Chinese===

我那件缀着银盘扣的灰褐色外套已经小了，上尉说我该去找他的裁缝做一件合身的，我完全同意。
“你昨晚睡得舒服不舒服，还用问么，”他说。“小弗雷德·皮姆普尔顿（皮姆普尔顿勋爵的次子）在那张床上睡了七个月——他赏光住我家那阵子；连他都满意，我不知道还有谁不满意。”
早饭过后我们出门逛城，菲茨西蒙斯先生把我引见给几位路上遇到的熟人，称我是他的忘年小友、沃特福德郡的雷德蒙先生；他又领我到他的帽店、裁缝店，把我介绍成前程远大、家产豪富的绅士；我明告裁缝，现钱只付一件——那件做得熨帖极了——他却执意给我做了好几件，我也不便推却。上尉自己也确实缺一身新行头，叫裁缝挑了一件漂亮的军式长礼服送到家里。
随后我们回家接菲茨西蒙斯太太，她乘着轿车去凤凰公园看阅兵；一群年轻绅士围着她转；她把我向他们一总引见，说我是她前一天的救命恩人。她那套夸奖实在到家，不出半个钟头，我已被当作全国最高门第的少年绅士、同所有主要贵族沾亲、菲茨西蒙斯上尉的表亲、岁入一万镑的继承人。菲茨西蒙斯说我家的田产他一寸一寸骑马量过；说真的，他既乐得替我编这些故事，我便由他——被众人捧着、充个大人物，我（人之常情）着实得意。我当时哪里想得到自己落进了一窝骗子——菲茨西蒙斯上尉不过是个冒险家，他的太太也是个没信用的角色；可青年人无时无刻不冒这种险，愿后来者以我为鉴。
我故意快快带过平生这段岁月：其中诸事辛酸，除我这不幸的人外没多大的兴味，同伴也实在配不上我的身份。

===Original===

The fact was, a young man could hardly have fallen into worse hands than those in which I now found myself. I have been to Donegal since, and have never seen the famous Castle of Fitzsimonsburgh, which is, likewise, unknown to the oldest inhabitants of that county; nor are the Granby Somersets much better known in Worcestershire. The couple into whose hands I had fallen were of a sort much more common then than at present, for the vast wars of later days have rendered it very difficult for noblemen’s footmen or hangers-on to procure commissions; and such, in fact, had been the original station of Captain Fitzsimons. Had I known his origin, of course I would have died rather than have associated with him: but in those simple days of youth I took his tales for truth, and fancied myself in high luck at being, at my outset into life, introduced into such a family. Alas! we are the sport of destiny. When I consider upon what small circumstances all the great events of my life have turned, I can hardly believe myself to have been anything but a puppet in the hands of Fate; which has played its most fantastic tricks upon me.
The Captain had been a gentleman’s gentleman, and his lady of no higher rank. The society which this worthy pair kept was at a sort of ordinary which they held, and at which their friends were always welcome on payment of a certain moderate sum for their dinner. After dinner, you may be sure that cards were not wanting, and that the company who played did not play for love merely. To these parties persons of all sorts would come: young bloods from the regiments garrisoned in Dublin: young clerks from the Castle; horse-riding, wine-tippling, watchman-beating men of fashion about town, such as existed in Dublin in that day more than in any other city with which I am acquainted in Europe. I never knew young fellows make such a show, and upon such small means. I never knew young gentlemen with what I may call such a genius for idleness; and whereas an Englishman with fifty guineas a year is not able to do much more than starve, and toil like a slave in a profession, a young Irish buck with the same sum will keep his horses, and drink his bottle, and live as lazy as a lord.

===Chinese===

实情是，一个青年人落到我这样的手里，差不多算落到了最坏的手里。我后来去过多尼戈尔，从没见到那座大名鼎鼎的菲茨西蒙斯堡；该郡最老的居民对它同样一无所知；格兰比·萨默塞特家在伍斯特郡的知名度也高不到哪儿去。我落入的这对夫妇，属于那个年头远比今日常见的一类人——后来的连年大战，使贵族的跟班和清客极难弄到军官委任状；而菲茨西蒙斯上尉当年干的本行，正是这一路。我若知道他的出身，当然宁死也不同他往来：可在那些单纯的少年岁月，我把他的鬼话当了真，庆幸自己一出道便进了这样一户人家，自以为鸿运高照。唉！我们都是命运的玩物。每念及我一生的重大转折全系于何等细小的事由，我简直不能不信，自己不过是命运手里的一具傀儡——它拿我变尽了戏法。
上尉原是某绅士的贴身跟班，他的太太出身也不高。这对宝贝交往的圈子，是他们开设的一家包饭桌：朋友们来吃饭，付一笔不贵的饭资，随到随吃。饭后，您放心，牌局少不了，同桌的也绝不是为爱而玩。到这些聚会上来的人三教九流：都柏林驻军的年轻军官；城堡里的年轻文书；纵马、酗酒、殴打更夫的城中浪荡子——这类人物，当年的都柏林比我所知的欧洲任何城市都多。我从没见过哪个年轻人这样讲排场，凭的家当这样薄；从没见过哪路青年绅士有这么一种可称之为游手好闲的天才：一个英国人年入五十几尼，除了挨饿和在职场上当牛做马，难有别的作为；一个年轻的爱尔兰阔少凭着同一笔钱，却养得起马，喝得起瓶装酒，活得像勋爵一样闲。

===Original===

Here was a doctor who never had a patient, cheek by jowl with an attorney who never had a client: neither had a guinea﻿—each had a good horse to ride in the Park, and the best of clothes to his back. A sporting clergyman without a living; several young wine-merchants, who consumed much more liquor than they had or sold; and men of similar character, formed the society at the house into which, by ill luck, I was thrown. What could happen to a man but misfortune from associating with such company?﻿—(I have not mentioned the ladies of the society, who were, perhaps, no better than the males)﻿—and in a very very short time I became their prey.
As for my poor twenty guineas, in three days I saw, with terror, that they had dwindled down to eight: theatres and taverns having already made such cruel inroads in my purse. At play I had lost, it is true, a couple of pieces; but seeing that everyone round about me played upon honour and gave their bills, I, of course, preferred that medium to the payment of ready money, and when I lost paid on account.
With the tailors, saddlers, and others, I employed similar means; and in so far Mr. Fitzsimons’s representation did me good, for the tradesmen took him at his word regarding my fortune (I have since learned that the rascal pigeoned several other young men of property), and for a little time supplied me with any goods I might be pleased to order. At length, my cash running low, I was compelled to pawn some of the suits with which the tailor had provided me; for I did not like to part with my mare, on which I daily rode in the Park, and which I loved as the gift of my respected uncle. I raised some little money, too, on a few trinkets which I had purchased of a jeweller who pressed his credit upon me; and thus was enabled to keep up appearances for yet a little time.
I asked at the post-office repeatedly for letters for Mr. Redmond, but none such had arrived; and, indeed, I always felt rather relieved when the answer of “No” was given to me; for I was not very anxious that my mother should know my proceedings in the extravagant life which I was leading at Dublin.

===Chinese===

这儿是一位从没有过病人的医生，同一位从没有过委托人的律师比邻而居：两个都没有一枚几尼——各人却有匹好马在公园里骑，有身上好的衣裳。一个没有圣职的赛马牧师；几位年轻的酒商，喝掉的酒远比他们有或卖的都多；还有些同一类的人物，构成了我误投的这个圈子的社会。同这号人厮混，除了招灾还能有什么好下场？——（我还没提这圈子里的女客，她们的成色怕也不比男客高）——不出几时，我便成了他们的猎物。
至于我那可怜的二十几尼，三天工夫，我眼睁睁看着它们缩成了八枚：戏园和酒馆已经在我钱袋上咬出了这样残忍的口子。牌桌上我确乎输了两枚；但见我周围人人凭荣誉打牌、一律打欠条，我当然也就偏向这种支付方式，输了便记账。
对裁缝、马具商等，我用的也是同一法子；就此而言，菲茨西蒙斯先生的吹嘘倒帮了我：商人们信了他替我编的身家（后来我知道，那无赖用同一手法宰过好几个有产的少年），有小小的几天，我定什么货他们供什么货。末了现钱见底，我只得把裁缝给做的几套衣裳典掉几套；母马我舍不得出手——我天天骑着它在公园里走，它是敬爱的舅舅所赠，我爱它。我又从一件首饰上押了点钱——那是从一个硬要赊给我的珠宝商手里买的；这样又勉强撑了几天场面。
我一趟趟到邮局问有没有雷德蒙先生的信，一封也没有；说实话，每回听到“没有”二字，我倒松一口气：我在都柏林过的这番挥霍日子，并不急着让母亲知道。

===Original===

It could not last very long, however; for when my cash was quite exhausted, and I paid a second visit to the tailor, requesting him to make me more clothes, the fellow hummed and ha’d, and had the impudence to ask payment for those already supplied: on which, telling him I should withdraw my custom from him, I abruptly left him. The goldsmith too (a rascal Jew) declined to let me take a gold chain to which I had a fancy; and I felt now, for the first time, in some perplexity. To add to it, one of the young gentlemen who frequented Mr. Fitzsimons’s boardinghouse had received from me, in the way of play, an I.O.U. for eighteen pounds (which I lost to him at piquet), and which, owing Mr. Curbyn, the livery-stable keeper, a bill, he passed into that person’s hands. Fancy my rage and astonishment, then, on going for my mare, to find that he positively refused to let me have her out of the stable, except under payment of my promissory note! It was in vain that I offered him his choice of four notes that I had in my pocket﻿—one of Fitzsimons’s for £20, one of Counsellor Mulligan’s, and so forth; the dealer, who was a Yorkshireman, shook his head, and laughed at every one of them; and said, “I tell you what, Master Redmond, you appear a young fellow of birth and fortune, and let me whisper in your ear that you have fallen into very bad hands﻿—it’s a regular gang of swindlers; and a gentleman of your rank and quality should never be seen in such company. Go home: pack up your valise, pay the little trifle to me, mount your mare, and ride back again to your parents﻿—it’s the very best thing you can do.”
In a pretty nest of villains, indeed, was I plunged! It seemed as if all my misfortunes were to break on me at once; for, on going home and ascending to my bedroom in a disconsolate way, I found the Captain and his lady there before me, my valise open, my wardrobe lying on the ground, and my keys in the possession of the odious Fitzsimons. “Whom have I been harbouring in my house?” roared he, as I entered the apartment. “Who are you, sirrah?”
“*Sirrah!* Sir,” said I, “I am as good a gentleman as any in Ireland.”
“You’re an impostor, young man: a schemer, a deceiver!” shouted the Captain.
“Repeat the words again, and I will run you through the body,” replied I.
“Tut, tut! I can play at fencing as well as you, Mr. **Redmond Barry**. Ah!

===Chinese===

这样的日子终究长不了；现钱一光，我二访裁缝，请他再添几件衣裳，那家伙支支吾吾，竟放肆到讨起先前那批的账来；我告诉他我要撤了他的生意，拂袖而去。金匠（一个犹太恶棍）也拒绝让我赊那条我中意的金链；我头一回感到了窘迫。雪上加霜的是，常来菲茨西蒙斯先生寄宿舍的一位少年绅士，牌桌上收过我一张十八镑的欠条（皮克牌输给他的）；他欠着代役马厩主人柯宾先生一笔账，便把我的欠条转到了那人手里。诸位想象我去取马时的怒火与错愕：他断然不放我的马出厩，除非清了欠条！我掏出袋里四张欠条任他挑——一张菲茨西蒙斯的二十镑，一张穆利根大律师的，等等——全没用；那马厩主是个约克郡人，对每张都摇头哂笑，说：“我跟你说，雷德蒙少爷，你瞧着是个有出身有家当的小伙子；我凑你耳朵边说句体己话——你落进极坏的一伙人手里了——整个是一窝子骗子；你这身份地位的绅士，不该叫人看见同这路人为伍。回家去：收拾行囊，把我这点小账清了，骑上你的马，回你父母身边去——这是你顶好的出路。”
我真是扎进了一窝漂亮的恶棍里！我的厄运仿佛约好了要一齐砸下来：回到家，灰心丧气地上楼进卧房，见上尉夫妇已先我而至，行囊敞着，衣裳摊了一地，钥匙握在那个可憎的菲茨西蒙斯手里。“我家里藏的到底是什么人？”我一进门他就吼。“你是什么东西，小子？”
“‘小子’！”我说，“我同爱尔兰任何一位绅士一样体面。”
“你是个骗子，年轻人：一个设局的、欺世的东西！”上尉嚷。
“再说一遍，我就捅你个透心凉。”我答。
“呸，呸！玩剑我可不输给你，**雷德蒙·巴里**先生。啊！

===Original===

you change colour, do you﻿—your secret is known, is it? You come like a viper into the bosom of innocent families; you represent yourself as the heir of my friends the Redmonds of Castle Redmond; I inthrojuice you to the nobility and genthry of this methropolis” (the Captain’s brogue was large, and his words, by preference, long); “I take you to my tradesmen, who give you credit, and what do I find? That you have pawned the goods which you took up at their houses.”
“I have given them my acceptances, sir,” said I with a dignified air.
“*Under what name*, unhappy boy﻿—under what name?” screamed Mrs. Fitzsimons; and then, indeed, I remembered that I had signed the documents Barry Redmond instead of Redmond Barry: but what else could I do? Had not my mother desired me to take no other designation? After uttering a furious tirade against me, in which he spoke of the fatal discovery of my real name on my linen﻿—of his misplaced confidence of affection, and the shame with which he should be obliged to meet his fashionable friends and confess that he had harboured a swindler, he gathered up the linen, clothes, silver toilet articles, and the rest of my gear, saying that he should step out that moment for an officer and give me up to the just revenge of the law.
During the first part of his speech, the thought of the imprudence of which I had been guilty, and the predicament in which I was plunged, had so puzzled and confounded me, that I had not uttered a word in reply to the fellow’s abuse, but had stood quite dumb before him. The sense of danger, however, at once roused me to action. “Hark ye, Mr. Fitzsimons,” said I; “I will tell you why I was obliged to alter my name: which is Barry, and the best name in Ireland. I changed it, sir, because, on the day before I came to Dublin, I killed a man in deadly combat﻿—an Englishman, sir, and a captain in His Majesty’s service; and if you offer to let or hinder me in the slightest way, the same arm which destroyed him is ready to punish you; and by Heaven, sir, you or I don’t leave this room alive!”
So saying, I drew my sword like lightning, and giving a “ha! ha!” and a stamp with my foot, lunged within an inch of Fitzsimons’s heart, who started back and turned deadly pale, while his wife, with a scream, flung herself between us.
“Dearest Redmond,” she cried, “be pacified.

===Chinese===

你变脸色了，是吧——你的把戏穿帮了，是吧？你像条毒蛇钻进清白人家的怀里；你冒充我朋友城堡雷德蒙雷德蒙家的继承人；我把你引荐给这座都会的贵族与绅士们”（上尉的土腔浓重，用词又偏爱长音节）“；我领你到我的商家那里赊货，结果呢？我发现你把他们给你的货全典当出去了。”
“我给他们的是承兑票据，先生。”我一脸尊严地说。
“用什么名字签的，可怜的孩子——用什么名字？”菲茨西蒙斯太太尖叫；这时我才想起，那些票据我签的是巴里·雷德蒙，不是雷德蒙·巴里：可不然我怎么办？母亲不是吩咐过我，不许用别的名字么？他冲我发了一通雷霆，说在我的内衣上发现了我的真名，说他的信任与情谊错付了人，说他将不得不羞愧地去面对他的时髦朋友、承认自家窝藏了一个骗子；骂罢，他把内衣、衣裳、银质梳妆用具连同我其余的家当一卷，说他这就出门叫警察，把我交给法律去正义地发落。
他那篇话的前半段里，我对自己闯下的荒唐、身陷的窘境又惊又乱，竟一个字没答他的骂，呆立如木鸡。可危险的知觉霎时把我激醒了。“听着，菲茨西蒙斯先生，”我说；“我来告诉你我为什么不得不改名：我本姓巴里，爱尔兰顶好的姓。我改姓，先生，是因为来都柏林的前一天，我在殊死决斗里杀了一个人——一个英格兰人，还是陛下军中的上尉；你若敢对我有半分刁难阻拦，斩了他这条胳膊照样办你；皇天在上，先生，今儿个不是你死就是我活！”
说罢我剑光一闪，“哈！哈！”一声跺脚，剑尖直逼菲茨西蒙斯心口一寸之内；他倒退一步，面如死灰，他太太一声尖叫，扑到我们中间。
“最亲爱的雷德蒙，”她喊，“息怒。

===Original===

Fitzsimons, you don’t want the poor child’s blood. Let him escape﻿—in Heaven’s name let him go.”
“He may go hang for me,” said Fitzsimons sulkily; “and he’d better be off quickly, too, for the jeweller and the tailor have called once, and will be here again before long. It was Moses the pawnbroker that peached: I had the news from him myself.” By which I conclude that Mr. Fitzsimons had been with the new laced frock-coat which he procured from the merchant tailor on the day when the latter first gave me credit.
What was the end of our conversation? Where was now a home for the descendant of the Barrys? Home was shut to me by my misfortune in the duel. I was expelled from Dublin by a persecution occasioned, I must confess, by my own imprudence. I had no time to wait and choose: no place of refuge to fly to. Fitzsimons, after his abuse of me, left the room growling, but not hostile; his wife insisted that we should shake hands, and he promised not to molest me. Indeed, I owed the fellow nothing; and, on the contrary, had his acceptance actually in my pocket for money lost at play. As for my friend Mrs. Fitzsimons, she sat down on the bed and fairly burst out crying. She had her faults, but her heart was kind; and though she possessed but three shillings in the world, and fourpence in copper, the poor soul made me take it before I left her﻿—to go﻿—whither? My mind was made up: there was a score of recruiting-parties in the town beating up for men to join our gallant armies in America and Germany; I knew where to find one of these, having stood by the sergeant at a review in the Phoenix Park, where he pointed out to me characters on the field, for which I treated him to drink.
I gave one of my shillings to Sullivan the butler of the Fitzsimonses, and, running into the street, hastened to the little alehouse at which my acquaintance was quartered, and before ten minutes had accepted His Majesty’s shilling. I told him frankly that I was a young gentleman in difficulties; that I had killed an officer in a duel, and was anxious to get out of the country. But I need not have troubled myself with any explanations; King George was too much in want of men then to heed from whence they came, and a fellow of my inches, the sergeant said, was always welcome. Indeed, I could not, he said, have chosen my time better.

===Chinese===

菲茨西蒙斯，你总不要这可怜孩子的血。放他走吧——看在老天爷份上，放他走。”
“让他上吊去吧，”菲茨西蒙斯悻悻地说；“他最好也快些滚，珠宝匠和裁缝已经来过一回，不久还要来。是当铺的摩西告的密：消息是我亲耳从他那儿听来的。”由此我断定，菲茨西蒙斯先生那件新的镶边礼服，就是商家头一天给我赊账那天，从裁缝那儿弄来的。
我们那场谈话怎样收的场？巴里家的子孙，如今何处是家？家门因决斗之祸对我关死了。都柏林呢——我必须承认——因我自己招来的一场围剿，把我驱逐了。我没有时间挑拣：没有一处避难的所在。菲茨西蒙斯骂够了我，嘟囔着出了屋，但无敌意；他太太非要我们握手，他答应不再为难我。说实话，我本不欠那家伙什么；相反，他打牌输的、我经手的欠条就在我袋里。至于我的朋友菲茨西蒙斯太太，她往床沿上一坐，索性放声大哭。她有她的毛病，心肠却是好的；她名下统共三先令，外加四便士铜板；这可怜人临别硬塞给了我——我去——去哪儿呢？我主意已定：城里正有一二十处募兵站，为我们在美洲和德意志的雄师招兵买马；其中一处我知道上哪儿找——凤凰公园阅兵时我同那位军士站在一起，他给我指点场上的人物，我请他喝过酒。
我把一枚先令给了菲茨西蒙斯家的管家沙利文，跑到街上，直奔我那位熟人驻扎的小酒馆，不出十分钟，就接下了陛下的先令。我对他直说，我是个落难的少年绅士；决斗杀了位军官，急着出国避祸。可这番解释本可省去：那时节乔治国王缺人缺得紧，没工夫问来路；我这副身量的汉子，军士说，走到哪儿都受欢迎。他还说，我这时机挑得再好不过。

===Original===

A transport was lying at Dunleary, waiting for a wind, and on board that ship, to which I marched that night, I made some surprising discoveries, which shall be told in the next chapter.

===Chinese===

一条运输船正泊在邓利里候风；当夜我便上了那条船——在船上我有几桩惊人的发现，容下章分解。

## V Barry Far from Military Glory / 五 巴里远离军功
===Original===

After the death of my protector, Captain Fagan, I am forced to confess that I fell into the very worst of courses and company. Being a rough soldier of fortune himself, he had never been a favourite with the officers of his regiment; who had a contempt for Irishmen, as Englishmen sometimes will have, and used to mock his brogue, and his blunt uncouth manners. I had been insolent to one or two of them, and had only been screened from punishment by his intercession; especially his successor, Mr. Rawson, had no liking for me, and put another man into the sergeant’s place vacant in his company after the battle of Minden. This act of injustice rendered my service very disagreeable to me; and, instead of seeking to conquer the dislike of my superiors, and win their goodwill by good behaviour, I only sought for means to make my situation easier to me, and grasped at all the amusements in my power. In a foreign country, with the enemy before us, and the people continually under contribution from one side or the other, numberless irregularities were permitted to the troops which would not have been allowed in more peaceable times. I descended gradually to mix with the sergeants, and to share their amusements: drinking and gambling were, I am sorry to say, our principal pastimes; and I fell so readily into their ways, that though only a young lad of seventeen, I was the master of them all in daring wickedness; though there were some among them who, I promise you, were far advanced in the science of every kind of profligacy. I should have been under the provost-marshal’s hands, for a dead certainty, had I continued much longer in the army: but an accident occurred which took me out of the English service in rather a singular manner.
The year in which George II died, our regiment had the honour to be present at the battle of Warburg (where the Marquis of Granby and his horse fully retrieved the discredit which had fallen upon the cavalry since Lord George Sackville’s defalcation at Minden), and where Prince Ferdinand once more completely defeated the Frenchmen. During the action, my lieutenant, Mr. Fakenham, of Fakenham, the gentleman who had threatened me, it may be remembered, with the caning, was struck by a musket-ball in the side.

===Chinese===

我的保护人费根上尉死后——恕我直言——我滑进了顶坏的行径和顶坏的伙伴堆里。他自己不过个粗豪的雇佣军人，在本团军官里向来不吃香；那些军官瞧不起爱尔兰人——英国人时有这种毛病——惯常学他的土腔，笑他粗直无文的举止。我对他们中一两个放肆过，全靠他说情才免了责罚；尤其是接他任的罗森先生，半点不喜欢我，明登一仗之后，把他连里出缺的军士位子给了别人。这桩不公把我的差事弄得面目可憎；我不但不设法化解上官的嫌恶、以好行为赢他们的好感，反而一心只想把自己的处境弄舒服些，能捞的消遣一样不落。身在异国，敌人当面，百姓被交战两边轮番搜刮，军纪松懈到平时绝不容许的地步。我渐渐降格同军士们厮混，分享他们的消遣：饮酒和赌博——说来惭愧——是我们主要的营生；我入他们的道入得那样快，虽只是个十七岁的小子，论胆大妄为，我是他们的祖师；虽说他们中间有几个，我告诉你，在各路荒唐的学问上早已登峰造极。倘使我再在军中呆下去，不用说是要落到宪兵司令手里去的：偏巧出了一桩意外，用一种相当别致的方式，把我从英军里拔了出去。
乔治二世驾崩的那一年，我们团有幸在瓦尔堡一战中列阵（格兰比侯爵和他的骑兵，在明登一仗乔治·萨克维尔勋爵渎职、败坏了骑兵名声之后，在这里把脸面挣了个囫囵），费迪南德亲王在那里又一次把法国人打得落花流水。战事进行中，我的中尉费克纳姆先生——就是那位，读者或许记得，威胁要用马鞭抽我的绅士——脉下中了一颗滑膛枪弹。

===Original===

He had shown no want of courage in this or any other occasion where he had been called upon to act against the French; but this was his first wound, and the young gentleman was exceedingly frightened by it. He offered five guineas to be carried into the town, which was hard by; and I and another man, taking him up in a cloak, managed to transport him into a place of decent appearance, where we put him to bed, and where a young surgeon (who desired nothing better than to take himself out of the fire of the musketry) went presently to dress his wound.
In order to get into the house, we had been obliged, it must be confessed, to fire into the locks with our pieces; which summons brought an inhabitant of the house to the door, a very pretty and black-eyed young woman, who lived there with her old half-blind father, a retired Jagdmeister of the Duke of Cassel, hard by. When the French were in the town, Meinherr’s house had suffered like those of his neighbours; and he was at first exceedingly unwilling to accommodate his guests. But the first knocking at the door had the effect of bringing a speedy answer; and Mr. Fakenham, taking a couple of guineas out of a very full purse, speedily convinced the people that they had only to deal with a person of honour.
Leaving the doctor (who was very glad to stop) with his patient, who paid me the stipulated reward, I was returning to my regiment with my other comrade﻿—after having paid, in my German jargon, some deserved compliments to the black-eyed beauty of Warburg, and thinking, with no small envy, how comfortable it would be to be billeted there﻿—when the private who was with me cut short my reveries by suggesting that we should divide the five guineas the lieutenant had given me.
“There is your share,” said I, giving the fellow one piece; which was plenty, as I was the leader of the expedition. But he swore a dreadful oath that he would have half; and when I told him to go to a quarter which I shall not name, the fellow, lifting his musket, hit me a blow with the butt-end of it, which sent me lifeless to the ground: when I awoke from my trance, I found myself bleeding with a large wound in the head, and had barely time to stagger back to the house where I had left the lieutenant, when I again fell fainting at the door.

===Chinese===

这一仗以及此前每一场同法国人交手的地方，他都不曾缺过胆气；可这是他头一回挂彩，这位年轻绅士吓得不轻。他出五个几尼，求人把他抬进就近的城里；我和另一个弟兄拿一件斗篷兜起他，把他弄进了一处看着体面的宅子，安顿上床，一位年轻军医——巴不得躲开枪林弹雨——随即来给他裹伤。
得承认，为进这宅子，我们先朝锁眼放了几枪；这一声召唤叫出了宅子里的一口人，一个非常标致、黑眼睛的年轻女子，同她半瞎的老父亲住在这里——老人是近处卡塞尔公爵手下退职的猎务官。法国人占城那阵，这位先生的宅子同邻居们的一样遭了殃；起初他极不情愿招待这几位客人。可头一顿敲门立刻敲出了结果；费克纳姆先生从一只鼓鼓的钱袋里摸出两枚几尼，很快叫这家人明白：同他们打交道的，是位体面人物。
医生（巴不得留下）陪着他的病人，病人把许下的赏钱付给了我。我同另一位弟兄往团里回去——临行还用我的德国土话，对瓦尔堡这位黑眼睛的美人献了几句当献的殷勤，心里不无艳羡地想：要是能在这里驻扎，该多受用——同路的那个大兵一句话打断了我的遐想：他说，中尉给我的那五个几尼，该两人分。
“这是你的一份。”我说，丢给他一枚；我是这一行的领队，给一枚尽够。可他赌了一个天杀的咒，非要一半不可；我叫他滚去一个我不便说出名字的地方，那家伙起滑膛枪，一枪托砸在我头上，把我打得人事不省。等我从昏迷里醒转，头上开了个大口子，淌着血；我挣扎着回到留下中尉的那宅子，刚到门口，又晕倒了。

===Original===

Here I must have been discovered by the surgeon on his issuing out; for when I awoke a second time I found myself in the ground-floor of the house, supported by the black-eyed girl, while the surgeon was copiously bleeding me at the arm. There was another bed in the room where the lieutenant had been laid﻿—it was that occupied by Gretel, the servant; while Lischen, as my fair one was called, had, till now, slept in the couch where the wounded officer lay.
“Who are you putting into that bed?” said he languidly, in German; for the ball had been extracted from his side with much pain and loss of blood.
They told him it was the corporal who had brought him.
“A corporal?” said he, in English; “turn him out.” And you may be sure I felt highly complimented by the words. But we were both too faint to compliment or to abuse each other much, and I was put to bed carefully; and, on being undressed, had an opportunity to find that my pockets had been rifled by the English soldier after he had knocked me down. However, I was in good quarters: the young lady who sheltered me presently brought me a refreshing drink; and, as I took it, I could not help pressing the kind hand that gave it me; nor, in truth, did this token of my gratitude seem unwelcome.
This intimacy did not decrease with further acquaintance. I found Lischen the tenderest of nurses. Whenever any delicacy was to be provided for the wounded lieutenant, a share was always sent to the bed opposite his, and to the avaricious man’s no small annoyance. His illness was long. On the second day the fever declared itself; for some nights he was delirious; and I remember it was when a commanding officer was inspecting our quarters, with an intention, very likely, of billeting himself on the house, that the howling and mad words of the patient overhead struck him, and he retired rather frightened. I had been sitting up very comfortably in the lower apartment, for my hurt was quite subsided; and it was only when the officer asked me, with a rough voice, why I was not at my regiment, that I began to reflect how pleasant my quarters were to me, and that I was much better here than crawling under an odious tent with a parcel of tipsy soldiers, or going the night-rounds or rising long before daybreak for drill.
The delirium of Mr. Fakenham gave me a hint, and I determined forthwith to *go mad*.

===Chinese===

想必是医生出门时发现了我；因为我第二次醒来，发现自己躺在宅子底层，黑眼睛姑娘扶着我，医生正对着我的胳膊大放其血。屋里，中尉躺着的那张床之外还有一张床——那是使女格蕾特尔睡的；我的美人儿名叫利欣，到方才为止，一直睡在伤员躺着的那张卧榻上。
“你们把谁往那张床上放？”他有气无力地用德语问；子弹是从他脉下取出来的，吃了大苦，流了不少血。
他们告诉他，是抬他来的那个下士。
“下士？”他用英语说，“把他撵出去。”诸位可想见，这话叫我觉得多受抬举。可我们两个都虚弱得厉害，恭维也罢，对骂也罢，都提不起来；我被小心地放上了床；脱衣裳时，我得以发现：我的口袋，已被那英国大兵在我打倒之后搜刮干净了。不过我的驻地不坏：收留我的年轻小姐即刻端来一杯提神的饮品；我接杯时，忍不住握了握递杯的那只温柔的手；说实在的，我这感激的凭证，看来也不讨她嫌。
这份亲密没有随着相熟而减退。我发现利欣是天下顶温柔的看护。但凡给伤员中尉备下什么可口的东西，对面那张床上总有一份送来，叫这位吝啬人大为光火。他的病拖得长。第二天热病发了；有几夜他满口谵语；我记得有一位指挥官来查我们的驻地——多半是想自己也驻到这宅子里来——头顶上病人的嚎叫和疯话把他吓着了，倒退着走了。我一直在楼下那间屋里舒舒服服坐着，我的伤早已平复；直到那军官粗声粗气问我为什么不在团里，我才开始掂量：这驻地于我多么可意；同喝醉的大兵们挤在讨厌的帐篷底下、夜里查哨、天不亮起来操练，哪比得上这里。
费克纳姆先生的谵语给了我一个点子，我当场拿定主意：装疯。

===Original===

There was a poor fellow about Brady’s Town called “Wandering Billy,” whose insane pranks I had often mimicked as a lad, and I again put them in practice. That night I made an attempt upon Lischen, saluting her with a yell and a grin which frightened her almost out of her wits; and when anybody came I was raving. The blow on the head had disordered my brain; the doctor was ready to vouch for this fact. One night I whispered to him that I was Julius Caesar, and considered him to be my affianced wife Queen Cleopatra, which convinced him of my insanity. Indeed, if Her Majesty had been like my Aesculapius, she must have had a carroty beard, such as is rare in Egypt.
A movement on the part of the French speedily caused an advance on our part. The town was evacuated, except by a few Prussian troops, whose surgeons were to visit the wounded in the place; and, when we were well, we were to be drafted to our regiments. I determined that I never would join mine again. My intention was to make for Holland, almost the only neutral country of Europe in those times, and thence to get a passage somehow to England, and home to dear old Brady’s Town.
If Mr. Fakenham is now alive, I here tender him my apologies for my conduct to him. He was very rich; he used me very ill. I managed to frighten away his servant who came to attend him after the affair of Warburg, and from that time would sometimes condescend to wait upon the patient, who always treated me with scorn; but it was my object to have him alone, and I bore his brutality with the utmost civility and mildness, meditating in my own mind a very pretty return for all his favours to me. Nor was I the only person in the house to whom the worthy gentleman was uncivil. He ordered the fair Lischen hither and thither, made impertinent love to her, abused her soups, quarrelled with her omelettes, and grudged the money which was laid out for his maintenance; so that our hostess detested him as much as, I think, without vanity, she regarded me.
For, if the truth must be told, I had made very deep love to her during my stay under her roof; as is always my way with women, of whatever age or degree of beauty.

===Chinese===

布雷迪镇上原有个可怜人，叫“游荡的比利”，他那疯癫的把戏，我小时候常学；如今我又拾了起来。当天夜里我朝利欣发作了一回，冲她一声怪叫、一咧嘴，几乎把她魂吓掉；谁来我都满口狂言。头上那一枪托震坏了我的脑子——医生乐于为此作证。一天夜里我对他耳语，说我是尤利乌斯·凯撒，并把他认作我订了婚的王后克莉奥佩特拉；这一下叫他确信我疯了。说真的，倘若王后陛下长得同我这位医神一样，那她必得有一部红萝卜色的胡子——埃及难得见的那种。
法军一动，我军随即推进。城里的人撤空了，只留下小队普鲁士（Prussian）部队，他们的军医负责探视城里的伤员；等我们养好了，就遣回各团。我拿定主意，再也不回我那个团。我打算投荷兰去——那年月几乎算欧洲（Europe）仅有的中立国——再从那里设法搭船去英格兰，回我亲爱的老布雷迪镇。
费克纳姆先生倘若如今还在世，我在此为我对他的行径向他告罪。他极有钱，待我极坏。瓦尔堡一仗之后他的仆人赶来伺候，叫我设法吓跑了；从那以后，我不时屈尊亲自照料病人——他从来待我以轻蔑；可我的目的正是单独同他在一起，我以极度的客气与温和受着他的粗暴，心里盘算着给他这一切“恩惠”一个非常漂亮的回敬。这位值价的先生在宅子里也不是只对我一人无礼。他差美丽的利欣前后奔走，对她轻薄，嫌她的汤，挑她的蛋卷的错，心疼花在他身上的每一文钱；于是我们的女主人恨他——我说这话不自夸——恨到同她看我差不多一般深。
因为，实情必须交代：住在她屋顶下的那些日子，我对她大动了爱情；这原是我对女人的老一套，不论年龄，不论姿色。

===Original===

To a man who has to make his way in the world, these dear girls can always be useful in one fashion or another; never mind, if they repel your passion; at any rate, they are not offended with your declaration of it, and only look upon you with more favourable eyes in consequence of your misfortune. As for Lischen, I told her such a pathetic story of my life (a tale a great deal more romantic than that here narrated﻿—for I did not restrict myself to the exact truth in that history, as in these pages I am bound to do), that I won the poor girl’s heart entirely, and, besides, made considerable progress in the German language under her instruction. Do not think me very cruel and heartless, ladies; this heart of Lischen’s was like many a town in the neighbourhood in which she dwelt, and had been stormed and occupied several times before I came to invest it; now mounting French colours, now green and yellow Saxon, now black and white Prussian, as the case may be. A lady who sets her heart upon a lad in uniform must prepare to change lovers pretty quickly, or her life will be but a sad one.
The German surgeon who attended us after the departure of the English only condescended to pay our house a visit twice during my residence; and I took care, for a reason I had, to receive him in a darkened room, much to the annoyance of Mr. Fakenham, who lay there: but I said the light affected my eyes dreadfully since my blow on the head; and so I covered up my head with clothes when the doctor came, and told him that I was an Egyptian mummy, or talked to him some insane nonsense, in order to keep up my character.
“What is that nonsense you were talking about an Egyptian mummy, fellow?” asked Mr. Fakenham peevishly.
“Oh! you’ll know soon, sir,” said I.
The next time that I expected the doctor to come, instead of receiving him in a darkened room, with handkerchiefs muffled, I took care to be in the lower room, and was having a game at cards with Lischen as the surgeon entered. I had taken possession of a dressing-jacket of the lieutenant’s, and some other articles of his wardrobe, which fitted me pretty well; and, I flatter myself, was no ungentlemanlike figure.
“Good-morrow, Corporal,” said the doctor, rather gruffly, in reply to my smiling salute.
“Corporal! Lieutenant, if you please,” answered I, giving an arch look at Lischen, whom I had instructed in my plot.

===Chinese===

对一个要在世上闯出路子的男人，这些亲爱的姑娘总有这样那样的用处；别管她们拒不相从；至少，你的表白不会开罪她们，她们反倒因为你这份不幸，拿更青眼的眼光看你。至于利欣，我把我的生平对她讲了一个顶哀婉的故事（比本书讲的浪漫得多——那一回我没有像这几页里必须做的那样，把自己限制在严格的事实里），赢得了这可怜姑娘的整个心；此外，还在她的教导下把德语学了个可观。女士们，别当我狠心没心肺；利欣这颗心，同她住的这一带许多城镇一样，在我来围攻之前，早已被攻克、被占领过好几回了：一会儿挂法国的旗，一会儿挂萨克森的黄绿旗，一会儿挂普鲁士的黑白旗，视情况而定。把心放在一个穿军装的少年身上的女士，必须准备着快快换情人，不然她这一生可有得愁。
英军开走后来照看我们的那位德国军医，在我驻留期间只屈尊光临本宅两回；我出于自己的一点道理，特意在一间遮暗的屋里见他——这叫躺在屋里的费克纳姆先生大为恼火；我说自挨了那一枪托，光线刺我眼睛刺得厉害；于是医生来时我就拿衣裳蒙住头，告诉他我是一具埃及木乃伊，或者对他说些疯话，好维持我的人设。
“你讲的那什么木乃伊的胡话是什么意思，家伙？”费克纳姆先生愠怒地问。
“哦！您很快就会知道，先生。”我说。
下一回料定医生要来，我不但不在遮暗的屋里蒙着手绢接他，反倒特意坐在楼下屋里，医生进门时正同利欣打牌。我占了中尉的一件晨衣，同他行头里的另外几件，穿着倒相当合身；我自诩，模样并不失绅士体统。
“日安，下士。”医生相当粗声地答我含笑的问好。
“中尉！劳驾。”我答，朝利欣淘气地递了个眼色——我的计谋她早有份。

===Original===

“How lieutenant?” asked the surgeon. “I thought the lieutenant was﻿—”
“Upon my word, you do me great honour,” cried I, laughing; “you mistook me for the mad corporal upstairs. The fellow has once or twice pretended to be an officer, but my kind hostess here can answer which is which.”
“Yesterday he fancied he was Prince Ferdinand,” said Lischen; “the day you came he said he was an Egyptian mummy.”
“So he did,” said the doctor; “I remember; but, ha! ha! do you know, Lieutenant, I have in my notes made a mistake in you two?”
“Don’t talk to me about his malady; he is calm now.”
Lischen and I laughed at this error as at the most ridiculous thing in the world; and when the surgeon went up to examine his patient, I cautioned him not to talk to him about the subject of his malady, for he was in a very excited state.
The reader will be able to gather from the above conversation what my design really was. I was determined to escape, and to escape under the character of Lieutenant Fakenham; taking it from him to his face, as it were, and making use of it to meet my imperious necessity. It was forgery and robbery, if you like; for I took all his money and clothes﻿—I don’t care to conceal it; but the need was so urgent, that I would do so again: and I knew I could not effect my escape without his purse, as well as his name. Hence it became my duty to take possession of one and the other.
As the lieutenant lay still in bed upstairs, I did not hesitate at all about assuming his uniform, especially after taking care to inform myself from the doctor whether any men of ours who might know me were in the town. But there were none that I could hear of; and so I calmly took my walks with Madame Lischen, dressed in the lieutenant’s uniform, made inquiries as to a horse that I wanted to purchase, reported myself to the commandant of the place as Lieutenant Fakenham, of Gale’s English regiment of foot, convalescent, and was asked to dine with the officers of the Prussian regiment at a very sorry mess they had. How Fakenham would have stormed and raged, had he known the use I was making of his name!

===Chinese===

“怎么是中尉？”军医问，“我记得中尉是——”
“哎呀，您太抬举我了，”我笑道，“您把我当成楼上那个疯下士了。那家伙装过一两回军官，不过哪位是哪位，我这位好房东太太作证。”
“昨天他还当自己是费迪南德亲王，”利欣说，“您来的那天，他说自己是埃及木乃伊。”
“正是，”医生说，“我记得；可是，哈！哈！您知道吗，中尉，我在笔记里把你们两个弄串了？”
“别对我讲他的病；他现在安静。”
利欣同我把这个差错当作天下第一滑稽事，笑了又笑；军医上楼查看他的病人时，我叮嘱他千万别对病人提他的病症，他正处在极度的亢奋里。
读者从上面这段对谈里，想必已猜出我真正的算盘。我决意逃走，而且以费克纳姆中尉的身份逃走；等于当着他的面把身份从他身上取走，拿来应我燃眉之急。您愿意，就管它叫伪造加抢劫；因为我卷走了他全部的钱和衣裳——我不屑隐瞒；可当时急需到那个地步，再来一回我也照做：我知道，没有他的钱袋连同他的名字，我逃不成。因此，把这两样都拿到手，就成了我的本分。
中尉既还躺在床上，我穿上他的军装全无顾虑，何况我已先向医生打听过，城里有没有可能认得我的我军的人。一个也没打听到；于是我穿着中尉的军装，安然同利欣夫人散步，探问我想买的那匹马，以“费克纳姆中尉，盖尔英格兰步兵团，伤病初愈”的身份向城防司令报到，还应普鲁士团军官之邀，去他们那伙寒酸的伙桌上吃了顿饭。费克纳姆若知道我把他的名字派了这样的用场，不知要怎样暴跳如雷！

===Original===

Whenever that worthy used to inquire about his clothes, which he did with many oaths and curses that he would have me caned at the regiment for inattention, I, with a most respectful air, informed him that they were put away in perfect safety below; and, in fact, had them very neatly packed, and ready for the day when I proposed to depart. His papers and money, however, he kept under his pillow; and, as I had purchased a horse, it became necessary to pay for it.
At a certain hour, then, I ordered the animal to be brought round, when I would pay the dealer for him. (I shall pass over my adieux with my kind hostess, which were very tearful indeed). And then, making up my mind to the great action, walked upstairs to Fakenham’s room attired in his full regimentals, and with his hat cocked over my left eye.
“You g*w*eat scound*w*el!” said he, with a multiplicity of oaths; “you mutinous dog! what do you mean by d*w*essing yourself in my *w*egimentals? As sure as my name’s Fakenham, when we get back to the *w*egiment, I’ll have your soul cut out of your body.”
“I’m promoted, Lieutenant,” said I, with a sneer. “I’m come to take my leave of you;” and then going up to his bed, I said, “I intend to have your papers and purse.” With this I put my hand under his pillow; at which he gave a scream that might have called the whole garrison about my ears. “Hark ye, sir!” said I, “no more noise, or you are a dead man!” and taking a handkerchief, I bound it tight around his mouth so as well-nigh to throttle him, and, pulling forward the sleeves of his shirt, tied them in a knot together, and so left him; removing the papers and the purse, you may be sure, and wishing him politely a good day.
“It is the mad corporal,” said I to the people down below who were attracted by the noise from the sick man’s chamber; and so taking leave of the old blind Jagdmeister, and an adieu (I will not say how tender) of his daughter, I mounted my newly purchased animal; and, as I pranced away, and the sentinels presented arms to me at the town-gates, felt once more that I was in my proper sphere, and determined never again to fall from the rank of a gentleman.

===Chinese===

那位值价的人物一问起他的衣裳——问时少不得赌咒，说回团后一定叫人鞭我个懈怠——我总是一脸恭顺，回禀说衣裳在楼下收得好好的；其实也真收得好好的，叠得整整齐齐，只等我动身的一天。他的文件和钱倒枕在他枕头底下；我既买了一匹马，就少不得付马钱。
于是在约好的时辰，我叫人把牲口牵到门前，当面付清马价。（我同好房东太太的离别，就不必细表了，泪可是流了个够。）然后，把心一横干那桩大事，我一身簇新的他那一团的全套军装，军帽歪在左眼上，走上楼进费克纳姆的房间。
“你、你这个恶棍！”他连着一串咒骂，“你这个犯上的狗！你穿我的军装是什么意思？只要我还叫费克纳姆，回了团，我要叫人把你的魂从你身子里剜出来。”
“我升官了，中尉。”我冷笑，“特来向您辞行。”说着走到他床前：“我要您的文件和钱袋。”说罢把手伸进他枕头底下；他发出一声能叫全城卫兵围到我耳朵边来的尖叫。“听着，先生！”我说，“再出声，你就是死人！”我拿一条手巾把他的嘴紧紧勒住，勒得他几乎背过气，又把他衬衫的两只袖子拉过来打个死结，就这样掠下他；文件和钱袋自然一并取走，还彬彬有礼地祝了他日安。
“是那个疯下士。”我对楼下被病人房里的动静引上来的人们说。随后辞别了老瞎猎务官，同他女儿道了别（多缠绵就不必说了），跨上新买的牲口；一路蹄声得得出城，城门的哨兵朝我举枪敬礼，我重新觉得回到了自己应在的圈子，暗暗立誓：再不从绅士的品级上跌下去了。

===Original===

I took at first the way towards Bremen, where our army was, and gave out that I was bringing reports and letters from the Prussian commandant of Warburg to headquarters; but, as soon as I got out of sight of the advanced sentinels, I turned bridle and rode into the Hesse-Cassel territory, which is luckily not very far from Warburg: and I promise you I was very glad to see the blue-and-red stripes on the barriers, which showed me that I was out of the land occupied by our countrymen. I rode to Hof, and the next day to Cassel, giving out that I was the bearer of despatches to Prince Henry, then on the Lower Rhine, and put up at the best hotel of the place, where the field-officers of the garrison had their ordinary. These gentlemen I treated to the best wines that the house afforded, for I was determined to keep up the character of the English gentleman, and I talked to them about my English estates with a fluency that almost made me believe in the stories which I invented. I was even asked to an assembly at Wilhelmshohe, the Elector’s palace, and danced a minuet there with the Hofmarshal’s lovely daughter, and lost a few pieces to his excellency the first huntmaster of his Highness.
At our table at the inn there was a Prussian officer who treated me with great civility, and asked me a thousand questions about England; which I answered as best I might. But this best, I am bound to say, was bad enough. I knew nothing about England, and the Court, and the noble families there; but, led away by the vaingloriousness of youth (and a propensity which I possessed in my early days, but of which I have long since corrected myself, to boast and talk in a manner not altogether consonant with truth), I invented a thousand stories which I told him; described the King and the Ministers to him, said the British Ambassador at Berlin was my uncle, and promised my acquaintance a letter of recommendation to him. When the officer asked me my uncle’s name, I was not able to give him the real name, and so said his name was O’Grady: it is as good a name as any other, and those of Kilballyowen, county Cork, are as good a family as any in the world, as I have heard. As for stories about my regiment, of these, of course, I had no lack. I wish my other histories had been equally authentic.

===Chinese===

我先取道去不来梅，我们大军驻扎之地，自称奉瓦尔堡普鲁士司令之命，给大营递呈报和信件；一俟前哨的哨兵看不见我，我拨转马头，骑进了黑森-卡塞尔地界——所幸那里离瓦尔堡不远：眼见拦木上蓝红相间的条纹，晓得已出了同胞们占领的地界，我告诉诸位，我心里着实欢喜。我骑到霍夫，次日到卡塞尔，自称是给下莱茵的亨利亲王送公文的信使，落脚在全城顶好的旅馆——驻军将官们包饭的地方。这几位先生，我拿店里顶好的酒款待；我决意把英国绅士的人设撑足，同他们大谈我的英国庄园，谈得那样溜，连我自己都快信了自己编的故事。我甚至被邀去选帝侯的宫邸威廉高地赴会，同宫务监可爱的女儿跳了一支小步舞，还输给殿下的首席猎务官大人几枚金币。
旅馆我们桌上有一位普鲁士军官，待我极为客气，向我打听了上千个关于英国的问题；我尽力作答。可这尽力——我得说——实在够呛。英国的事，宫廷的事，世族的事，我一概不知；可年少的好虚荣（再加上我早年有一样毛病——爱吹，说话同真事不大合辙，这毛病后来我早已改了）引着我一路编下去，给他编了上千个故事；给他描画了国王和各位大臣，说英国驻柏林（Berlin）大使是我伯父，还许我这位新相识一封荐书。等他问起我伯父的姓氏，我一时说不出个真的，便说他姓奥格雷迪：这个姓不比哪个姓差；科克郡基尔巴利奥文的那一支，据我所闻，也不比天下任何世家差。至于我们团里的故事，那自然有的是。只朊我别的历史也同这些一样确凿。

===Original===

On the morning I left Cassel, my Prussian friend came to me with an open smiling countenance, and said he, too, was bound for Dusseldorf, whither I said my route lay; and so laying our horses’ heads together we jogged on. The country was desolate beyond description. The prince in whose dominions we were was known to be the most ruthless seller of men in Germany. He would sell to any bidder, and during the five years which the war (afterwards called the Seven Years’ War) had now lasted, had so exhausted the males of his principality, that the fields remained untilled: even the children of twelve years old were driven off to the war, and I saw herds of these wretches marching forwards, attended by a few troopers, now under the guidance of a red-coated Hanovarian sergeant, now with a Prussian sub-officer accompanying them; with some of whom my companion exchanged signs of recognition.
“It hurts my feelings,” said he, “to be obliged to commune with such wretches; but the stern necessities of war demand men continually, and hence these recruiters whom you see market in human flesh. They get five-and-twenty dollars from our Government for every man they bring in. For fine men﻿—for men like you,” he added, laughing, “we would go as high as a hundred. In the old King’s time we would have given a thousand for you, when he had his giant regiment that our present monarch disbanded.”
“I knew one of them,” said I, “who served with you: we used to call him Morgan Prussia.”
“Indeed; and who was this Morgan Prussia?”
“Why, a huge grenadier of ours, who was somehow snapped up in Hanover by some of your recruiters.”
“The rascals!” said my friend: “and did they dare take an Englishman?”
“ ’Faith this was an Englishman, and a great deal too sharp for them; as you shall hear. Morgan was taken, then, and drafted into the giant guard, and was the biggest man almost among all the giants there. Many of these monsters used to complain of their life, and their caning, and their long drills, and their small pay; but Morgan was not one of the grumblers. ‘It’s a deal better,’ said he, ‘to get fat here in Berlin, than to starve in rags in Tipperary!’ ”
“Where is Tipperary?” asked my companion.
“That is exactly what Morgan’s friends asked him.

===Chinese===

离开卡塞尔那个早晨，我的普鲁士朋友一脸开朗的笑意来寻我，说他也去杜塞尔多夫——我说我的路线正是往那儿；于是两匹马并辔而行。乡野凋敝得没法形容。我们所在的这位亲王的封地，是全德意志出了名的最狠的人贩子。谁出价他卖给谁；这场仗（后来称作七年战争）打到如今第五个年头，他封地里的男丁已叫他卖得精光，田地荒着没人种：连十二岁的孩子也被驱去当兵；我看见一队队这样的可怜虫往前开，押送的不过几名骑兵，一会儿是个红衣的汉诺威军士领着，一会儿是个普鲁士下级军官伴着；其中有几个，我同伴同他们互打了个照面的手势。
“同这班可怜虫打交道，”他说，“我心里也难受；可战争的铁需要源源不断的人，所以才有你见的这些拿人肉做买卖的募兵贩子。他们每带进来一个，政府给二十五块银元。遇到好汉——像您这样的，”他笑着补一句，“我们出到一百。老王在世那阵，您这样的我们肯出一千——他那巨人团，如今的君王把它裁撤了。”
“那里面有一个我认得，”我说，“在你们军中当过差：我们从前叫他‘摩根·普鲁士’。”
“当真；这位摩根·普鲁士是谁？”
“嗦，我们那儿一个大个子的掷弹兵，不知怎么在汉诺威叫你们的募兵贩子捞了去。”
“那班流氓！”我朋友说，“他们竟敢动英国人？”
“千真万确是个英国人，而且精得叫他们没法办；您听我说。摩根被掳去，编进巨人卫队，在那群巨人里几乎数他块头最大。那些巨人怪物，多有怨命的：怨日子、怨鞭子、怨没完的操练、怨饷银薄；摩根可不是发牢骚的。‘在这儿柏林养膘，’他说，‘总比破衣烂衫在蒂珀雷里挨饿强！’”
“蒂珀雷里在哪儿？”我同伴问。
“这正是摩根的朋友们问他的。

===Original===

It is a beautiful district in Ireland, the capital of which is the magnificent city of Clonmel: a city, let me tell you, sir, only inferior to Dublin and London, and far more sumptuous than any on the Continent. Well, Morgan said that his birthplace was near that city, and the only thing which caused him unhappiness, in his present situation, was the thought that his brothers were still starving at home, when they might be so much better off in His Majesty’s service.
“ ‘ ’Faith,’ says Morgan to the sergeant, to whom he imparted the information, ‘it’s my brother Bin that would make the fine sergeant of the guards, entirely!’
“ ‘Is Ben as tall as you are?’ asked the sergeant.
“ ‘As tall as *me*, is it? Why, man, I’m the shortest of my family! There’s six more of us, but Bin’s the biggest of all. Oh! out and out the biggest. Seven feet in his stockin-*fut*, as sure as my name’s Morgan!’
“ ‘Can’t we send and fetch them over, these brothers of yours?’
“ ‘Not you. Ever since I was seduced by one of you gentlemen of the cane, they’ve a mortal aversion to all sergeants,’ answered Morgan: ‘but it’s a pity they cannot come, too. What a monster Bin would be in a grenadier’s cap!’
“He said nothing more at the time regarding his brothers, but only sighed as if lamenting their hard fate. However, the story was told by the sergeant to the officers, and by the officers to the King himself; and His Majesty was so inflamed by curiosity, that he actually consented to let Morgan go home in order to bring back with him his seven enormous brothers.”
“And were they as big as Morgan pretended?” asked my comrade. I could not help laughing at his simplicity.
“Do you suppose,” cried I, “that Morgan ever came back? No, no; once free, he was too wise for that. He has bought a snug farm in Tipperary with the money that was given him to secure his brothers; and I fancy few men of the guards ever profited so much by it.”
The Prussian captain laughed exceedingly at this story, said that the English were the cleverest nation in the world, and, on my setting him right, agreed that the Irish were even more so. We rode on very well pleased with each other; for he had a thousand stories of the war to tell, of the skill and gallantry of Frederick, and the thousand escapes, and victories, and defeats scarcely less glorious than victories, through which the King had passed.

===Chinese===

那是爱尔兰（Ireland）一个顶美的郡，首府是气象宏丽的克朗梅尔城：那座城，先生，容我说一句，只逊于都柏林（Dublin）和伦敦，比大陆上哪座城都阔气。总之，摩根说他老家就在那城边上；他如今境地里唯一不痛快的，是想起几个兄弟还在家里挨饿，其实到国王陛下麾下当差，日子不知好到哪里去。
“‘真的，’摩根对那个军士说——这心思就对他一个人交了底，‘我兄弟宾天生是当卫队军士的好料，十足的好料！’
“‘宾同你一般高么？’军士问。
“‘同我一般高，你说的！嗦，老兄，我在家里还是最矮的一个！我们家还有六个，宾是顶大的那个。哦，压倒一切的大。七英尺挂零，穿袜子量的，就像我叫摩根一样真！’
“‘能不能打发人去把你这些兄弟接来？’
“‘接不来。自打我叫你们这班拄手杖的老爷拐了一个，他们见了军士就死恨，’摩根答，‘可惜他们来不成。宾戴上掷弹兵帽，该是多大的一个怪物！’
“当时他关于兄弟没再多说，只是叹气，像哀他们的苦命。可这故事军士传给了军官，军官传到了国王本人耳朵里；陛下好奇心大动，竟真的准了摩根回乡，去把他那七个巨汉兄弟接回来。”
“他们真有摩根吹的那么大么？”我同伴问。他的天真叫我忍不住笑。
“您当，”我喊，“摩根还回来么？不，不；一旦脱身，他精明得很，才不回来。他拿给他去接兄弟的那笔钱，在蒂珀雷里置了一座舒舒服服的田庄；卫队里靠这个发财的，我估摸没有第二个。”
普鲁士上尉听这故事笑了个痛快，说英国人是天下最灵的民族；经我纠正，又同意爱尔兰人更灵。我们并辔前行，彼此都十分相得：他有上千个战争故事，腓特烈的韬略与胆气，国王身经的千百次脱险、千百场大捷、以及那不比大捷逊色多少的败仗。

===Original===

Now that I was a gentleman, I could listen with admiration to these tales: and yet the sentiment recorded at the end of the last chapter was uppermost in my mind but three weeks back, when I remembered that it was the great general got the glory, and the poor soldier only insult and the cane.
“By the way, to whom are you taking despatches?” asked the officer.
It was another ugly question, which I determined to answer at haphazard; and so I said “To General Rolls.” I had seen the general a year before, and gave the first name in my head. My friend was quite satisfied with it, and we continued our ride until evening came on; and our horses being weary, it was agreed that we should come to a halt.
“There is a very good inn,” said the Captain, as we rode up to what appeared to me a very lonely-looking place.
“This may be a very good inn for Germany,” said I, “but it would not pass in old Ireland. Corbach is only a league off: let us push on for Corbach.”
“Do you want to see the loveliest woman in Europe?” said the officer. “Ah! you sly rogue, I see *that* will influence you;” and, truth to say, such a proposal *was* always welcome to me, as I don’t care to own. “The people are great farmers,” said the Captain, “as well as innkeepers;” and, indeed, the place seemed more a farm than an inn yard. We entered by a great gate into a Court walled round, and at one end of which was the building, a dingy ruinous place. A couple of covered waggens were in the court, their horses were littered under a shed hard by, and lounging about the place were some men and a pair of sergeants in the Prussian uniform, who both touched their hats to my friend the Captain.
