The first days of a marriage are commonly very trying; and I have known couples, who lived together like turtledoves for the rest of their lives, peck each other’s eyes out almost during the honeymoon. I did not escape the common lot; in our journey westward my Lady Lyndon chose to quarrel with me because I pulled out a pipe of tobacco (the habit of smoking which I had acquired in Germany when a soldier in Billow’s, and could never give it over), and smoked it in the carriage; and also her Ladyship chose to take umbrage both at Ilminster and Andover, because in the evenings when we lay there I chose to invite the landlords of the Bell and the Lion to crack a bottle with me. Lady Lyndon was a haughty woman, and I hate pride; and I promise you that in both instances I overcame this vice in her. On the third day of our journey I had her to light my pipematch with her own hands, and made her deliver it to me with tears in her eyes; and at the Swan Inn at Exeter I had so completely subdued her, that she asked me humbly whether I would not wish the landlady as well as the host to step up to dinner with us. To this I should have had no objection, for, indeed, Mrs. Bonnyface was a very good-looking woman; but we expected a visit from my Lord Bishop, a kinsman of Lady Lyndon, and the *bienséances* did not permit the indulgence of my wife’s request. I appeared with her at evening service, to compliment our right reverend cousin, and put her name down for twenty-five guineas, and my own for one hundred, to the famous new organ which was then being built for the cathedral.

===Chinese===

南下哈克顿堡——我们家族在德文郡最大、最古老的祖业——的一路，都按王国头等人物的身份，缓慢而庄重地行进。一名穿我号衣的前导骑马先行，一镇一镇替我们预订宿处；于是我们在安德弗、伊尔明斯特、埃克塞特一路摆驾驻驿；第四天傍晚，赶在晚饭前到了那座古色古香的男爵府邸前，府门是那种可憎的哥特式趣味，准能叫沃尔波尔先生欢喜得发狂。
新婚头几天照例最难熬；我见过两口子，后半辈子过得像斑鸠一样和美，蜜月里却几乎把彼此的眼珠子啄了出来。我也没能逃过这个通例：西行路上，林登伯爵夫人（Lady Lyndon）挑了由头同我吵，因为我掏出烟斗（这习惯是我在德国比洛团里当兵时养上的，再也戒不掉），在马车里抽了；在伊尔明斯特和安德弗，她又挑了由头动气，因为我们歇宿的那些晚上，我请了“钟”饭店和“狮”饭店的店主上来同我干瓶酒。林登伯爵夫人是个高傲的女人，我恨骄傲；我担保诸位，这两回我都治好了她这毛病。旅程第三天，我叫她亲手给我点烟媒，叫她含着眼泪递给我；在埃克塞特的“天鹅”客栈，我已把她降服得服服帖帖，她竟怯生生问我，愿不愿叫老板娘也同老板一道上楼陪我们吃饭。这我本无不可——说真的，邦尼费斯太太是位很耐看的女人；只是我们候着林登伯爵夫人的亲戚主教大人的驾，体统（bienséances）上不容我应承太太的这个请求。我陪她出席了晚祷，给我们那位尊敬的堂亲增光，为当时大教堂正建造的著名新管风琴捐了款：她的名字记了二十五几尼，我的一百。

===Original===

This conduct, at the very outset of my career in the county, made me not a little popular; and the residentiary canon, who did me the favour to sup with me at the inn, went away after the sixth bottle, hiccuping the most solemn vows for the welfare of such a p-p-pious gentleman.
Before we reached Hackton Castle, we had to drive through ten miles of the Lyndon estates, where the people were out to visit us, the church bells set a-ringing, the parson and the farmers assembled in their best by the roadside, and the school children and the labouring people were loud in their hurrahs for her Ladyship. I flung money among these worthy characters, stopped to bow and chat with his reverence and the farmers, and if I found that the Devonshire girls were among the handsomest in the kingdom is it my fault? These remarks my Lady Lyndon especially would take in great dudgeon; and I do believe she was made more angry by my admiration of the red cheeks of Miss Betsy Quarringdon of Clumpton, than by any previous speech or act of mine in the journey. “Ah, ah, my fine madam, you are jealous, are you?” thought I, and reflected, not without deep sorrow, how lightly she herself had acted in her husband’s lifetime, and that those are most jealous who themselves give most cause for jealousy.
Round Hackton village the scene of welcome was particularly gay: a band of music had been brought from Plymouth, and arches and flags had been raised, especially before the attorney’s and the doctor’s houses, who were both in the employ of the family. There were many hundreds of stout people at the great lodge, which, with the park-wall, bounds one side of Hackton Green, and from which, for three miles, goes (or rather went) an avenue of noble elms up to the towers of the old castle. I wished they had been oak when I cut the trees down in ’79, for they would have fetched three times the money: I know nothing more culpable than the carelessness of ancestors in planting their grounds with timber of small value, when they might just as easily raise oak. Thus I have always said that the Roundhead Lyndon of Hackton, who planted these elms in Charles II’s time, cheated me of ten thousand pounds.

===Chinese===

这番做派，在我立足本郡之初，就替我赚得不少人心；那位赏脸到客栈陪我夜饮的驻堂法政牧师，第六瓶下肚之后告辞，一路打着嗝，为这样一位虔、虔、虔诚的绅士的最庄严的福祉许愿不止。
未到哈克顿堡，我们先驶过十英里的林登家产业，乡民倾村出来迎，教堂的钟敲响了，牧师和农户穿戴一新聚在路旁，学堂的孩子和庄稼人给他们的伯爵夫人喝彩喝得震天响。我朝这些可敬的人物撒钱，停下来同牧师大人和农户们鞠躬寒暄；倘若我发现德文郡的姑娘属于全王国最标致的一类，难道是我的错？这几句评论，林登伯爵夫人听着尤其恼火；我深信，我夸克伦普顿的贝琪·夸林登小姐红扑扑的脸蛋，比路上我任何言语行动都更叫她动怒。“啊哈，我的好太太，你吃醋了，是么？”我心想，并且——不无沉痛地——想起她自己当年待丈夫何等轻浮；想起最会吃醋的，正是自己最给人吃醋由头的那些人。
哈克顿村四围，欢迎的场面格外热闹：从普利茅斯请来了一班乐师，扎了牌楼，挂了彩旗——律师和医生家门前尤其齐全，两位都在这家吃俸。大门口聚着好几百健壮的乡民；那道大门连同庄园围墙，界着哈克顿绿野的一边，从那里起，一条（不如说曾经是一条）三英里的堂皇榆树大道，直通到古堡的塔楼。七九年我砍那些树的时候，倒情愿它们是橡树，能卖三倍的价：祖先们种地，尽掠不值钱的木材种，明明种橡树一样容易，我看没有比这更该责备的了。所以我一向说，查理二世年间种下这批榆树的哈克顿圆颅党林登，坑了我一万镑。

===Original===

For the first few days after our arrival, my time was agreeably spent in receiving the visits of the nobility and gentry who came to pay their respects to the noble new-married couple, and, like Bluebeard’s wife in the fairy tale, in inspecting the treasures, the furniture, and the numerous chambers of the castle. It is a huge old place, built as far back as Henry V’s time, besieged and battered by the Cromwellians in the Revolution, and altered and patched up, in an odious old-fashioned taste, by the Roundhead Lyndon, who succeeded to the property at the death of a brother whose principles were excellent and of the true Cavalier sort, but who ruined himself chiefly by drinking, dicing, and a dissolute life, and a little by supporting the King. The castle stands in a fine chase, which was prettily speckled over with deer; and I can’t but own that my pleasure was considerable at first, as I sat in the oak parlour of summer evenings, with the windows open, the gold and silver plate shining in a hundred dazzling colours on the sideboards, a dozen jolly companions round the table, and could look out over the wide green park and the waving woods, and see the sun setting on the lake, and hear the deer calling to one another.
The exterior was, when I first arrived, a quaint composition of all sorts of architecture; of feudal towers, and gable-ends in Queen Bess’s style, and rough-patched walls built up to repair the ravages of the Roundhead cannon: but I need not speak of this at large, having had the place new-faced at a vast expense, under a fashionable architect, and the façade laid out in the latest French-Greek and most classical style. There had been moats, and drawbridges, and outer walls; these I had shaved away into elegant terraces, and handsomely laid out in parterres according to the plans of Monsieur Cornichon, the great Parisian architect, who visited England for the purpose.

===Chinese===

到后的头几天，我的光阴过得惬意：一是接待前来向贵门新婚夫妇致敬的贵族与乡绅；二是学童话里蓝胡子的妻子，检视古堡的珍藏、家具和众多的房间。这是一所庞大的老宅，远建于亨利五世年间，革命内战中叫克伦威尔党人围攻过、轰击过；后来又由圆颅党林登按一种可憎的老派趣味修修补补——他是在他那位原则极好、真正的骑士党兄弟死后接下的产业；那位兄弟主要毁于豪饮、掷骰和荒唐，小半毁于保王。古堡立在一座漂亮的猎苑里，苑中斑斑点点掠着鹿；我不得不认，起初我的乐趣着实不小：夏日的黄昏坐在橡木客厅里，窗子敞着，金银器皿在餐具柜上闪出千百种耀眼的光，桌边围着一打快活的伙伴，望出去是宽阔的绿苑和起伏的林涛，看太阳落进湖里，听鹿群彼此呦鸣。
我初到时，外观是各种建筑式样的一锅怪烩：封建的塔楼，伊丽莎白女王样式的山墙端头，还有为修补圆颅党炮火创痕而胡乱补砌的粗墙：这些不必细表——我后来花巨资，请了位时兴的建筑师，把整个门面翻新，正立面布置成最新的法国希腊式，最古典的样式。原先有壕沟、吊桥和外郭墙；我把它统统削成雅致的台地，按大巴黎建筑师科尔尼雄先生的设计，铺成漂亮的花坛——他为此专程来过英国。

===Original===

After ascending the outer steps, you entered an antique hall of vast dimensions, wainscoted with black carved oak, and ornamented with portraits of our ancestors: from the square beard of Brook Lyndon, the great lawyer in Queen Bess’s time, to the loose stomacher and ringlets of Lady Saccharissa Lyndon, whom Vandyck painted when she was a maid of honour to Queen Henrietta Maria, and down to Sir Charles Lyndon, with his riband as a knight of the Bath; and my Lady, painted by Hudson, in a white satin sack and the family diamonds, as she was presented to the old King George II. These diamonds were very fine: I first had them reset by Boehmer when we appeared before their French Majesties at Versailles; and finally raised £18,000 upon them, after that infernal run of ill luck at Goosetree’s, when Jemmy Twitcher (as we called my Lord Sandwich), Carlisle, Charley Fox, and I played hombre for four-and-forty hours *sans désemparer*. Bows and pikes, huge stag-heads and hunting implements, and rusty old suits of armour, that may have been worn in the days of Gog and Magog for what I know, formed the other old ornaments of this huge apartment; and were ranged round a fireplace where you might have turned a coach-and-six. This I kept pretty much in its antique condition, but had the old armour eventually turned out and consigned to the lumber-rooms upstairs; replacing it with china monsters, gilded settees from France, and elegant marbles, of which the broken noses and limbs, and ugliness, undeniably proved their antiquity: and which an agent purchased for me at Rome. But such was the taste of the times (and, perhaps, the rascality of my agent), that thirty thousand pounds’ worth of these gems of art only went for three hundred guineas at a subsequent period, when I found it necessary to raise money on my collections.
From this main hall branched off on either side the long series of staterooms, poorly furnished with high-backed chairs and long queer Venice glasses, when first I came to the property; but afterwards rendered so splendid by me, with the gold damasks of Lyons and the magnificent Gobelin tapestries I won from Richelieu at play.

===Chinese===

登上一段外部台阶，先入一座古旧的大厅，极宽敞，四壁黑橡木雕板，饰着我们祖先的画像：从伊丽莎白女王年间那位大律师布鲁克·林登的方须，到凡·戴克笔下的萨克查丽莎·林登夫人的松身胸衣和长卷发——她当年是亨利埃塔玛丽亚王后的女官；直到查尔斯·林登爵士，佩着巴斯骑士的绶带；还有我夫人，赫德森笔下，白缎长袍、一身家传钻石——她觐见老王乔治二世时便是这副装束。这批钻石极好：我们在凡尔赛觐见法国王上王后之前，我先叫伯默尔重新镶过；末了，在鹅树俱乐部那场要命的倒运之后，我拿它们押了一万八千镑——那一回，杰米·特威彻（我们这样称呼桑德威奇勋爵）、卡莱尔、查利·福克斯同我，四十四个钟头不停手地打翁布尔牌。弓箭长矛、巨大的鹿头和猎具、锈蚀的旧甲胄——戈格玛戈格年代穿没穿过我可不知道——是这间大堂其余的旧装饰；一概陈列在一座壁炉四周，那炉膛大得能转过一辆六马大车。这一间我大体保存古貌，只是旧甲胄后来叫人搬掉，发落到楼上贮物间去了，换上瓷兽、法国的镀金长椅、雅致的大理石雕——那些残鼻断臂和丑陋，无可辩驳地证明了它们的古老；是位代理人在罗马替我买的。可当年时尚如此（也许还有我代理人的刁滑），这些艺术珍品值三万镑，后来我须得用藏品筹钱时，只卖了三百几尼。
从正厅往两边，分出一长列正房；我初接产业时，里头只有高背椅和又长又怪的威尼斯镜，陈设寒酸；后来经我一手装点得金碧辉煌：里昂的金缎，还有我从黎塞留赌桌上赢来的豪迈的戈布兰挂毯。

===Original===

There were thirty-six bedrooms *de maître*, of which I only kept three in their antique condition﻿—the haunted room as it was called, where the murder was done in James II’s time, the bed where William slept after landing at Torbay, and Queen Elizabeth’s stateroom. All the rest were redecorated by Cornichon in the most elegant taste; not a little to the scandal of some of the steady old country dowagers; for I had pictures of Boucher and Vanloo to decorate the principal apartments, in which the Cupids and Venuses were painted in a manner so natural, that I recollect the old wizened Countess of Frumpington pinning over the curtains of her bed, and sending her daughter, Lady Blanche Whalebone, to sleep with her waiting-woman, rather than allow her to lie in a chamber hung all over with looking-glasses, after the exact fashion of the Queen’s closet at Versailles.
For many of these ornaments I was not so much answerable as Cornichon, whom Lauraguais lent me, and who was the intendant of my buildings during my absence abroad. I had given the man carte blanche, and when he fell down and broke his leg, as he was decorating a theatre in the room which had been the old chapel of the castle, the people of the country thought it was a judgment of Heaven upon him. In his rage for improvement the fellow dared anything. Without my orders he cut down an old rookery which was sacred in the country, and had a prophecy regarding it, stating, “When the rook-wood shall fall, down goes Hackton Hall.” The rooks went over and colonised Tiptoff Woods, which lay near us (and be hanged to them!), and Cornichon built a temple to Venus and two lovely fountains on their site. Venuses and Cupids were the rascal’s adoration: he wanted to take down the Gothic screen and place Cupids in our pew there; but old Doctor Huff the rector came out with a large oak stick, and addressed the unlucky architect in Latin, of which he did not comprehend a word, yet made him understand that he would break his bones if he laid a single finger upon the sacred edifice. Cornichon made complaints about the “Abbé Huff,” as he called him. (“*Et quel abbé, grand Dieu!*” added he, quite bewildered, “*un abbé avec douze enfans*”); but I encouraged the Church in this respect, and bade Cornichon exert his talents only in the castle.

===Chinese===

主卧室有三十六间，我只留了三间原样：一间是所谓闹鬼的屋子，詹姆斯二世年间在那里出过人命；一张床，是威廉在托尔贝登陆后睡过的；还有伊丽莎白女王的正房。其余的都由科尔尼雄以最雅致的趣味重新装饰过——惹得几位稳重老派的乡村太夫人相当侧目；因为我拿布歇和凡·洛的画装点主要的房间，画上的小爱神和维纳斯画得那样逼真，我记得皱巴巴的老弗拉姆平顿伯爵夫人，赶忙在她床帏上别帘子，还打发她的女儿布兰奇·韦尔博恩夫人去同侍女睡，只因不肯让女儿睡在一间四壁全是镜子的屋里——那镜子排布的样式，同凡尔赛王后的私室一模一样。
这些装点，多半不能怪我，该怪科尔尼雄——他是拉劳盖借给我的，我旅居国外期间，由他总管我的营造。我给了这人全权；有一回他布置一间剧场——用的是古堡旧礼拜堂改的屋子——失足跌断了腿，乡人皆以为是上天对他的报应。这家伙改良成瘾，什么都敢。他不待我吩咐，擅自砍掉一片老白嘴鸦巢林——那林子在当地是神圣的，还有一句谶语：“鸦林一倒，哈克顿堂倒。”白嘴鸦飞过河去，殖民到了近处的蒂普托夫林子（叫它们见鬼去！），科尔尼雄在原地盖了一座维纳斯庙、两座可爱的喷泉。维纳斯和小爱神是这无赖的偶像：他想把教堂的哥特式隔屏拆掉，往我们的家族席位里安小爱神；老教务牧师哈夫博士提着一根大橡木棍出来，用拉丁文对付那倒霉的建筑师——建筑师一个字不懂，却全明白了：他若敢碰圣所一根指头，就打断他的骨头。科尔尼雄告这位“阿夫神父”的状。（“而且好一位神父，老天爷！”他补一句，简直糊涂了，“一个神父，养着十二个孩子！”）可在这件事上我站在教会一边，吩咐科尔尼雄只在古堡里施展本事。

===Original===

There was a magnificent collection of ancient plate, to which I added much of the most splendid modern kind; a cellar which, however well furnished, required continual replenishing, and a kitchen which I reformed altogether. My friend, Jack Wilkes, sent me down a cook from the Mansion House, for the English cookery﻿—the turtle and venison department: I had a chef (who called out the Englishman, by the way, and complained sadly of the *gros cochon* who wanted to meet him with *coups de poing*) and a couple of aides from Paris, and an Italian confectioner, as my *officiers de bouche*. All which natural appendages to a man of fashion, the odious, stingy old Tiptoff, my kinsman and neighbour, affected to view with horror; and he spread through the country a report that I had my victuals cooked by Papists, lived upon frogs, and, he verily believed, fricasseed little children.
But the squires ate my dinners very readily for all that, and old Doctor Huff himself was compelled to allow that my venison and turtle were most orthodox. The former gentry I knew how to conciliate, too, in other ways. There had been only a subscription pack of foxhounds in the county and a few beggarly couples of mangy beagles, with which old Tiptoff pattered about his grounds; I built a kennel and stables, which cost £30,000, and stocked them in a manner which was worthy of my ancestors, the Irish kings. I had two packs of hounds, and took the field in the season four times a week, with three gentlemen in my hunt-uniform to follow me, and open house at Hackton for all who belonged to the hunt.
These changes and this *train de vivre* required, as may be supposed, no small outlay; and I confess that I have little of that base spirit of economy in my composition which some people practise and admire. For instance, old Tiptoff was hoarding up his money to repair his father’s extravagance and disencumber his estates; a good deal of the money with which he paid off his mortgages my agent procured upon mine. And, besides, it must be remembered I had only a life-interest upon the Lyndon property, was always of an easy temper in dealing with the money-brokers, and had to pay heavily for insuring her Ladyship’s life.
At the end of a year Lady Lyndon presented me with a son﻿—Bryan Lyndon I called him, in compliment to my royal ancestry: but what more had I to leave him than a noble name?

===Chinese===

家里原有一批古代金银器，琳琅可观；我又添了许多最华丽的近代精品；酒窖的家底无论如何厚，也须不断补充；厨房则由我彻底改了制。我的朋友杰克·威尔克斯从市长公馆荐来一位厨子，专管英国菜——甲鱼和鹿肉这一门；我又从巴黎请来一位大厨（顺便说一句，他把那英国厨子骂了个狗血淋头，直叹那头非要同他挥拳头的肥猪），带两名下手，外加一位意大利糖果师，凑成我的饮馔官班。这一切时髦人的天然排场，我那位可憎、抠门的老亲戚兼近邻蒂普托夫（Tiptoff），偏要装出恐惧的样子看不上；他在乡里放风，说我的饭菜是教皇党徒做的，我吃的是青蛙，而且——他深信——还炖小孩吃。
可乡绅们照样吃我的饭吃得香，连老哈夫博士也不得不承认，我的鹿肉和甲鱼最合正教。对这些旧日缙绅，我另有一套笼络之法。本郡原先只有一份集股的狐犬群，另加几对生癞的劣种小猎犬——老蒂普托夫拿它们在他园子里嗒嗒地跑；我盖起狗舍马厩，花掉三万镑，按配得上我祖先爱尔兰诸王的气派充实起来。我养了两群猎犬，猎季每礼拜出猎四回，身后跟着三名穿猎装的绅士，哈克顿对全猎会的人敞开大门。
这些改动、这套排场，可想而知，须得不小的开销；我承认，我天性里缺那种有些人奉行、有些人钦佩的卑贱的节约精神。比方说，老蒂普托夫攒钱，为的是弥补他父亲的挥霍、赎清产业的押款；他赎押的那许多钱，相当一部分是我的代理人拿我的产业抵押筹来的。再者，别忘了：我对林登产业只有终身权益；同钱贩子打交道我一向随和；为夫人保寿险，我又付了重价。
满一年时，林登伯爵夫人给我生了个儿子——我唤他布莱恩·林登（Bryan），以我的王系血统致意：可除了一样高贵的姓氏，我还有什么留给他？

===Original===

Was not the estate of his mother entailed upon the odious little Turk, Lord Bullingdon? and whom, by the way, I have not mentioned as yet, though he was living at Hackton, consigned to a new governor. The insubordination of that boy was dreadful. He used to quote passages of *Hamlet* to his mother, which made her very angry. Once when I took a horsewhip to chastise him, he drew a knife, and would have stabbed me: and, ’faith, I recollected my own youth, which was pretty similar; and, holding out my hand, burst out laughing, and proposed to him to be friends. We were reconciled for that time, and the next, and the next; but there was no love lost between us, and his hatred for me seemed to grow as he grew, which was apace.
I determined to endow my darling boy Bryan with a property, and to this end cut down twelve thousand pounds’ worth of timber on Lady Lyndon’s Yorkshire and Irish estates: at which proceeding Bullingdon’s guardian, Tiptoff, cried out, as usual, and swore I had no right to touch a stick of the trees; but down they went; and I commissioned my mother to repurchase the ancient lands of Ballybarry and Barryogue, which had once formed part of the immense possessions of my house. These she bought back with excellent prudence and extreme joy; for her heart was gladdened at the idea that a son was born to my name, and with the notion of my magnificent fortunes.
To say truth, I was rather afraid, now that I lived in a very different sphere from that in which she was accustomed to move, lest she should come to pay me a visit, and astonish my English friends by her bragging and her brogue, her rouge and her old hoops and furbelows of the time of George II: in which she had figured advantageously in her youth, and which she still fondly thought to be at the height of the fashion. So I wrote to her, putting off her visit; begging her to visit us when the left wing of the castle was finished, or the stables built, and so forth. There was no need of such precaution. “A hint’s enough for me, Redmond,” the old lady would reply. “I am not coming to disturb you among your great English friends with my old-fashioned Irish ways. It’s a blessing to me to think that my darling boy has attained the position which I always knew was his due, and for which I pinched myself to educate him. You must bring me the little Bryan, that his grandmother may kiss him, one day.

===Chinese===

他母亲的产业，不是限定继承在那可憎的土耳其小崽子布林登（Bullingdon）勋爵名下么？说来这人我至今还没提过，虽然他就住在哈克顿，交给了一位新任的监护人。那孩子桀骜得可怕。他惯对他母亲念《哈姆雷特》的段子，惹得她直冒火。有一回我拿起马鞭要教训他，他抽出一把刀，真要捅我；说真的，我想起自己的少年时代，同这几乎一个样；我伸出手去，放声大笑，提议同他言和。那一次和了，下一次又和了，再一次又和了；可我们之间没有半点情分；他一天天长，对我的恨也一天天长，长得快。
我决意给我心爱的儿子布莱恩置一份产业，为此在伯爵夫人约克郡和爱尔兰（Irish）的产业上，砍下一万二千镑的树木；这一手照例惹得布林登的监护人蒂普托夫大喊大叫，赌咒说我无权动一根枝子；可树照样倒了；我委托我母亲，把巴利巴里和巴里奥格的旧地赎买回来——那里原是我家庞大产业的一部分。她以极好的审慎、极大的欢喜办妥了：一想到我的名下有了儿子，想到我的豪富，她的心就亮堂。
说真的，如今我活动的圈子同她素来行走的天差地远，我倒真怕她上门来看我，怕她的吹嘘和土腔、她的胭脂、她乔治二世年间的旧裙环和褶裥吓着我的英国朋友——她年轻时靠这一身穿戴出过风头，至今还痴心当它时髦。于是我写信给她，请她缓一缓：等古堡的西翼落成，或马厩盖好，再来看我们。这样的周全原是多余。“一点我就明白了，雷德蒙，”老太太回信说，“我不去，不拿我这爱尔兰老派搅了你的英国大朋友。想到我的心肝儿子得到了我一直知道他该得的位置——为供他读书我掏空了自己——我就有福气了。你得把小布莱恩给我带来，好叫他祖母亲他一口，哪一天。

===Original===

Present my respectful blessing to her Ladyship his mamma. Tell her she has got a treasure in her husband, which she couldn’t have had had she taken a duke to marry her; and that the Barrys and the Bradys, though without titles, have the best of blood in their veins. I shall never rest until I see you Earl of Ballybarry, and my grandson Lord Viscount Barryogue.”
How singular it was that the very same ideas should be passing in my mother’s mind and my own! The very title she had pitched upon had also been selected (naturally enough) by me; and I don’t mind confessing that I had filled a dozen sheets of paper with my signature, under the names of Ballybarry and Barryogue, and had determined with my usual impetuosity to carry my point. My mother went and established herself at Ballybarry, living with the priest there until a tenement could be erected, and dating from “Ballybarry Castle;” which, you may be sure, I gave out to be a place of no small importance. I had a plan of the estate in my study, both at Hackton and in Berkeley Square, and the plans of the elevation of Ballybarry Castle, the ancestral residence of Barry Lyndon, Esq., with the projected improvements, in which the castle was represented as about the size of Windsor, with more ornaments to the architecture; and eight hundred acres of bog falling in handy, I purchased them at three pounds an acre, so that my estate upon the map looked to be no insignificant one.5
I also in this year made arrangements for purchasing the Polwellan estate and mines in Cornwall from Sir John Trecothick, for £70,000﻿—an imprudent bargain, which was afterwards the cause to me of much dispute and litigation. The troubles of property, the rascality of agents, the quibbles of lawyers, are endless. Humble people envy us great men, and fancy that our lives are all pleasure. Many a time in the course of my prosperity I have sighed for the days of my meanest fortune, and envied the boon companions at my table, with no clothes to their backs but such as my credit supplied them, without a guinea but what came from my pocket; but without one of the harassing cares and responsibilities which are the dismal adjuncts of great rank and property.

===Chinese===

请替我给我家夫人大人请安。告诉她，她得的这个丈夫是件宝，她就是嫁个公爵也得不着这样的；告诉她，巴里家和布雷迪家虽说没有爵位，血管里流的却是天下最好的血。不见你当上巴利巴里伯爵、我孙子当上巴里奥格子爵，我死不瞑目。”
真怪：我母亲心里转的，竟同我心里的一个样！她挑的名号，我（自然得很）也早挑中了；我不妨招认：我拿“巴利巴里”和“巴里奥格”两个署名，写满过十几张纸，并且照我素来的火爆脾气，拿定主意非办到不可。我母亲去巴利巴里住了下来，先同那里的神父一起住，等宅子盖起来，信封上落款“巴利巴里堡”——诸位放心，我对外说这是非同小可的地方。我在哈克顿的书房和伯克利广场的书房里，各挂了一张产业总图，还有巴里·林登（Barry Lyndon）绅士祖居“巴利巴里堡”的立面图连同规划中的扩建——图上那座堡画得约莫温莎堡一般大，建筑装饰还更多些；又赶上八百英亩沼地廉价出手，我按每英亩三镑买了下来，于是我的产业在地图上瞧着着实不算小。5
也是这一年，我安排着从约翰·特雷科西克爵士手里买下康沃尔的波尔韦兰田产和矿场，价钱七万镑——一桩欠考虑的买卖，日后给我惹来数不清的争执和官司。产业的烦恼、代理人的刁滑、律师的绕脖子话，是没有底的。小百姓艳羡我们大人物，只当我们的日子全是快活。得意的年月里，我不止一次叹念我走霉运的时日，艳羡我自己餐桌上的酒肉朋友——身上除我赊给他们的没有别的穿戴，口袋里除我给的没有一个几尼，却也没有一样缠人的心事和责任，那些东西才是高门第大产业阴惨的附庸。

===Original===

I did little more than make my appearance, and assume the command of my estates, in the kingdom of Ireland; rewarding generously those persons who had been kind to me in my former adversities, and taking my fitting place among the aristocracy of the land. But, in truth, I had small inducements to remain in it after having tasted of the genteeler and more complete pleasures of English and Continental life; and we passed our summers at Buxton, Bath, and Harrogate, while Hackton Castle was being beautified in the elegant manner already described by me, and the season at our mansion in Berkeley Square.
It is wonderful how the possession of wealth brings out the virtues of a man; or, at any rate, acts as a varnish or lustre to them, and brings out their brilliancy and colour in a manner never known when the individual stood in the cold grey atmosphere of poverty. I assure you it was a very short time before I was a pretty fellow of the first class; made no small sensation at the coffeehouses in Pall Mall and afterwards at the most famous clubs. My style, equipages, and elegant entertainments were in everybody’s mouth, and were described in all the morning prints. The needier part of Lady Lyndon’s relatives, and such as had been offended by the intolerable pomposity of old Tiptoff, began to appear at our routs and assemblies; and as for relations of my own, I found in London and Ireland more than I had ever dreamed of, of cousins who claimed affinity with me. There were, of course, natives of my own country (of which I was not particularly proud), and I received visits from three or four swaggering shabby Temple bucks, with tarnished lace and Tipperary brogue, who were eating their way to the bar in London; from several gambling adventurers at the watering-places, whom I soon speedily let to know their place; and from others of more reputable condition. Among them I may mention my cousin the Lord Kilbarry, who, on the score of his relationship, borrowed thirty pieces from me to pay his landlady in Swallow Street; and whom, for my own reasons, I allowed to maintain and credit a connection for which the Heralds’ College gave no authority whatsoever.

===Chinese===

在爱尔兰王国，我只走了一趟，接掌了我的产业：慷慨酬谢了当年我落难时善待过我的人，在当地的贵人间占了我的位置。但说实在的，尝过英格兰和大陆上更雅更齐全的乐趣之后，留在那里的诱因很小；我们的夏天在巴克斯顿、巴斯和哈罗盖特度过，那时节哈克顿堡正按我描述的雅致路数装点，社交季则在我们伯克利广场的宅子里度过。
财富这东西能把人的美德显出来，真叫人惊叹；至少，能给美德上一层漆、一层光，把它们的亮泽和色彩映出来——人立在贫穷那冷灰的空气里时，那是从不见的。我担保诸位，没过多久，我就是第一等的漂亮人物了；在蓓尔美尔的咖啡馆，随后在最出名的俱乐部里，轰动不小。我的做派、车马、雅宴，挂在每个人嘴上，各家晨报都有描画。林登伯爵夫人那一边穷些的亲戚，还有那些被老蒂普托夫那副臭架子开罪了的人，渐渐出现在我们的晚会和舞会上；至于我自家的亲戚，我在伦敦和爱尔兰找到的表亲之多，做梦也没梦见过，个个都来认我。其中自然有我的同胞（我并不特别引以为荣）：三四位在伦敦法学院啃着面包往律师路上爬、花边发乌、满口蒂珀雷里土腔的摆阔穷小子，来拜访过我；赛马场和海水浴场上的几位赌棍冒险家也来过——我很快叫他们认清了自己的位置；还有几位体面些的。其中不妨一提我表兄基尔巴里勋爵：他凭着亲戚名分，借了我三十枚去付燕子街的房租；而我，出于自己的一点道理，由他维持并宣扬这层连纹章院都不认账的亲戚关系。

===Original===

Kilbarry had a cover at my table; punted at play, and paid when he liked, which was seldom; had an intimacy with, and was under considerable obligations to, my tailor; and always boasted of his cousin the great Barry Lyndon of the West country.
Her Ladyship and I lived, after a while, pretty separate when in London. She preferred quiet: or to say the truth, I preferred it; being a great friend to a modest tranquil behaviour in woman, and a taste for the domestic pleasures. Hence I encouraged her to dine at home with her ladies, her chaplain, and a few of her friends; admitted three or four proper and discreet persons to accompany her to her box at the opera or play on proper occasions; and indeed declined for her the too frequent visits of her friends and family, preferring to receive them only twice or thrice in a season on our grand reception days. Besides, she was a mother, and had great comfort in the dressing, educating, and dandling our little Bryan, for whose sake it was fit that she should give up the pleasures and frivolities of the world; so she left *that* part of the duty of every family of distinction to be performed by me. To say the truth, Lady Lyndon’s figure and appearance were not at this time such as to make for their owner any very brilliant appearance in the fashionable world. She had grown very fat, was shortsighted, pale in complexion, careless about her dress, dull in demeanour; her conversations with me characterised by a stupid despair, or a silly blundering attempt at forced cheerfulness still more disagreeable: hence our intercourse was but trifling, and my temptations to carry her into the world, or to remain in her society, of necessity exceedingly small. She would try my temper at home, too, in a thousand ways. When requested by me (often, I own, rather roughly) to entertain the company with conversation, wit, and learning, of which she was a mistress: or music, of which she was an accomplished performer, she would as often as not begin to cry, and leave the room. My company from this, of course, fancied I was a tyrant over her; whereas I was only a severe and careful guardian over a silly, bad-tempered, and weak-minded lady.

===Chinese===

基尔巴里在我桌上有个席位；赌钱下注，高兴才还——难得高兴；同我的裁缝交情深，欠着人家不少；逢人便夸他表弟、西国的伟人巴里·林登。
夫人同我在伦敦，过一阵便各过各的了。她喜欢清静：或者说实话，是我喜欢她清静；我一向赞成女人端庄娴静、安于家室之乐。所以我鼓励她在家同她的女官们、她的牧师、几位朋友吃饭；准三四位规矩妥当的人在适当的日子陪她去包厢听戏；说真的，我还替她回绝了她娘家和朋友们过于频繁的走动，情愿一个社交季里只在我们盛大的接待日见他们两三回。再说，她已是做母亲的人，装扮、教导、逗弄我们的小布莱恩，于她是极大的安慰；为着孩子，她也该舍弃尘世的浮华了；于是每个体面人家都有的那一半职分，就由我替她代劳了。说句实话，林登伯爵夫人这时节的身段容貌，已难替主人撑起社交场上的门面。她长得很胖，近视，脸色苍白，衣着随便，举止沉闷；同我说话，不是一股死心眼的绝望，便是一股硬挤出来的傻气的快活，更叫人败兴：因此我们的往来寥寥，我带她出门、陪她在一处的兴致，自然也小得很。在家里她也千百样地磨我的性子。我（我认，常常相当粗声）请她拿她的谈吐、机锋和学问款待客人——她原是此中高手——或弹一手琴——她的演奏极见功夫——她十有八九是哭起来，离席而去。我的客人由此自然当我虐待她；其实我不过是一位糊涂、坏脾气、没主见的女主的严厉而尽责的监护人。

===Original===

She was luckily very fond of her youngest son, and through him I had a wholesome and effectual hold of her; for if in any of her tantrums or fits of haughtiness﻿—(this woman was intolerably proud; and repeatedly, at first, in our quarrels, dared to twit me with my own original poverty and low birth)﻿—if, I say, in our disputes she pretended to have the upper hand, to assert her authority against mine, to refuse to sign such papers as I might think necessary for the distribution of our large and complicated property, I would have Master Bryan carried off to Chiswick for a couple of days; and I warrant me his lady-mother could hold out no longer, and would agree to anything I chose to propose. The servants about her I took care should be in my pay, not hers: especially the child’s head nurse was under *my* orders, not those of my lady; and a very handsome, red-cheeked, impudent jade she was; and a great fool she made me make of myself. This woman was more mistress of the house than the poor-spirited lady who owned it. She gave the law to the servants; and if I showed any particular attention to any of the ladies who visited us, the slut would not scruple to show her jealousy, and to find means to send them packing. The fact is, a generous man is always made a fool of by some woman or other, and this one had such an influence over me that she could turn me round her finger.6
Her infernal temper (Mrs. Stammer was the jade’s name) and my wife’s moody despondency, made my house and home not over-pleasant: hence I was driven a good deal abroad, where, as play was the fashion at every club, tavern, and assembly, I, of course, was obliged to resume my old habit, and to commence as an amateur those games at which I was once unrivalled in Europe. But whether a man’s temper changes with prosperity, or his skill leaves him when, deprived of a confederate, and pursuing the game no longer professionally, he joins in it, like the rest of the world, for pastime, I know not; but certain it is, that in the seasons of 1774﻿–﻿75 I lost much money at White’s and the Cocoa-Tree, and was compelled to meet my losses by borrowing largely upon my wife’s annuities, insuring her Ladyship’s life, and so forth.

===Chinese===

亏得她极疼小儿子，我借这孩子拿住了她，拿得稳当而有效：每逢她使性子、摆架子——（这女人骄傲得叫人受不了；起初我们拌嘴，她不止一回敢拿我本来的穷和低出身戳我）——我是说，每逢争执里她想占上风，想拿她的权威压我的，拒绝签我分发那份庞大繁杂的家业所必需的文书，我就叫人把小布莱恩抱去奇斯威克两天；我管保他这位母亲大人撑不了多久，我提什么她应什么。她身边的仆人，我留意都拿我的钱、不听她的：尤其是孩子的乳母，只听我的、不听夫人的——好一个标致、红脸皮、没规矩的浪蹄子；她害我出的洋相可不小。这女人在这宅子里，比那位没脾气的正牌女主更做得了主。仆人的规矩由她定；我若对来访的女客献半点殷勤，那荡妇必吃醋给你看，变着法子把客人打发走。事实是，大方的男人总要栽在某个女人手里；这一个对我影响之大，能把我攦在手指上转。6
她那副要命的脾气（那蹄子名叫斯坦默太太），加上我太太阴郁的消沉，叫我的家宅说不上舒心：于是多半时候我被赶到外头；外头呢，俱乐部、酒馆、集会处处以赌为尚，我自然只得重掠旧业，以票友的身份重开当年独步欧洲（Europe）的那些牌局。可是一个人的脾气是不是随境遇变，他的手上功夫是不是离了同伙、不再以赌为业、只同世人一样消遣时便离了他，我说不上；只是千真万确：1774至75年两个社交季，我在怀特和可可树输了许多钱，不得不靠大举抵押夫人的年金、替夫人保寿险等等来填窟窿。

===Original===

The terms at which I raised these necessary sums and the outlays requisite for my improvements were, of course, very onerous, and clipped the property considerably; and it was some of these papers which my Lady Lyndon (who was of a narrow, timid, and stingy turn) occasionally refused to sign: until I *persuaded* her, as I have before shown.
My dealings on the turf ought to be mentioned, as forming part of my history at this time; but, in truth, I have no particular pleasure in recalling my Newmarket doings. I was infernally bit and bubbled in almost every one of my transactions there; and though I could ride a horse as well as any man in England, was no match with the English noblemen at backing him. Fifteen years after my horse, Bay Bülow, by Sophy Hardcastle, out of Eclipse, lost the Newmarket stakes, for which he was the first favourite, I found that a noble earl, who shall be nameless, had got into his stable the morning before he ran; and the consequence was that an outside horse won, and your humble servant was out to the amount of fifteen thousand pounds. Strangers had no chance in those days on the heath: and, though dazzled by the splendour and fashion assembled there, and surrounded by the greatest persons of the land﻿—the royal dukes, with their wives and splendid equipages; old Grafton, with his queer bevy of company, and such men as Ancaster, Sandwich, Lorn﻿—a man might have considered himself certain of fair play and have been not a little proud of the society he kept; yet, I promise you, that, exalted as it was, there was no set of men in Europe who knew how to rob more genteelly, to bubble a stranger, to bribe a jockey, to doctor a horse, or to arrange a betting-book. Even *I* couldn’t stand against these accomplished gamesters of the highest families in Europe. Was it my own want of style, or my want of fortune? I know not. But now I was arrived at the height of my ambition, both my skill and my luck seemed to be deserting me. Everything I touched crumbled in my hand; every speculation I had failed, every agent I trusted deceived me. I am, indeed, one of those born to make, and not to keep fortunes; for the qualities and energy which lead a man to effect the first are often the very causes of his ruin in the latter case: indeed, I know of no other reason for the misfortunes which finally befell me.

===Chinese===

筹这些必需款子同我各项改造开销的利息条件，自然苛刻，把产业削去了不少；其中有些文书，林登伯爵夫人（她天生小性、胆怯、抠门）偶尔拒签——直到我像前面演示的那样，把她“劝”服。
我在赛马场上的买卖也该提一笔，算这一时期历史的一部分；可说实话，我并没多大兴致回顾我在纽马基特的业绩。那里我几乎桩桩生意都叫人结结实实地宰了坑了；虽说骑马我不输英格兰任何人，可相马下注，同英国的贵族们比就不是一个对手。我的马“海湾比洛”——索菲·哈德卡斯尔所生，父系“日蚀”——输了纽马基特大奖，它是头一号热门；十五年后我才知道，一位不具名的伯爵在赛前早晨进过它的马房；结果一匹冷马赢了，鄙人折了一万五千镑。那些年在那片草原上，生人没有半分机会：虽说叫那儿的煊赫时髦耀花了眼，同全国头等人物为伍——皇家公爵们带着夫人和华美的车乘；老格拉夫顿带着他那伙古怪的相与；还有安卡斯特、桑德威奇、洛恩这路人——人尽可以当自己稳拿公道，还颇为结交的圈子得意；可我告诉你，那个圈子尽管高，全欧洲却没有哪一伙人比他们更文雅地劫、更利落地坑生人、更会买通骑师、更会给马下药、更会排赌账。连我也敌不过这些全欧洲最高门第出来的全才赌客。是我自己欠风度，还是欠运气？我不知道。总之如今我攀到了雄心的顶点，本事和运气却像是一齐离开了我。我摸什么，什么在我手里碎；每一桩投机都砸，每一个信托的代理人都骗我。我实在是那种天生会挣家、不会守家的人：叫人挣成第一桩的那些品质和精力，往往正是叫他在第二桩上翻船的原因；说真话，最后落到我头上的那些灾殃，我找不出别的解释。

===Original===

7
I had always a taste for men of letters, and perhaps, if the truth must be told, have no objection to playing the fine gentleman and patron among the wits. Such people are usually needy, and of low birth, and have an instinctive awe and love of a gentleman and a laced coat; as all must have remarked who have frequented their society. Mr. Reynolds, who was afterwards knighted, and certainly the most elegant painter of his day, was a pretty dexterous courtier of the wit tribe; and it was through this gentleman, who painted a piece of me, Lady Lyndon, and our little Bryan, which was greatly admired at the Exhibition (I was represented as quitting my wife, in the costume of the Tippleton Yeomanry, of which I was major; the child starting back from my helmet like what-d’ye-call’im﻿—Hector’s son, as described by Mr. Pope in his *Iliad*); it was through Mr. Reynolds that I was introduced to a score of these gentlemen, and their great chief, Mr. Johnson. I always thought their great chief a great bear. He drank tea twice or thrice at my house, misbehaving himself most grossly; treating my opinions with no more respect than those of a schoolboy, and telling me to mind my horses and tailors, and not trouble myself about letters. His Scotch bear-leader, Mr. Boswell, was a butt of the first quality. I never saw such a figure as the fellow cut in what he called a Corsican habit, at one of Mrs. Cornely’s balls, at Carlisle House, Soho. But that the stories connected with that same establishment are not the most profitable tales in the world, I could tell tales of scores of queer doings there. All the high and low demireps of the town gathered there, from his Grace of Ancaster down to my countryman, poor Mr. Oliver Goldsmith the poet, and from the Duchess of Kingston down to the Bird of Paradise, or Kitty Fisher. Here I have met very queer characters, who came to queer ends too: poor Hackman, that afterwards was hanged for killing Miss Reay, and (on the sly) his Reverence Doctor Simony, whom my friend Sam Foote, of the Little Theatre, bade to live even after forgery and the rope cut short the unlucky parson’s career.
It was a merry place, London, in those days, and that’s the truth. I’m writing now in my gouty old age, and people have grown vastly more moral and matter-of-fact than they were at the close of the last century, when the world was young with me.

===Chinese===

7
我一向喜欢文人，倘若非说不可，我还不反对在才子堆里充一充漂亮人物和恩主。这路人多半穷，出身低，对绅士、对镶绦子的衣裳怀着本能的敬畏与爱慕——常同他们往来的人想必都见过。雷诺兹先生——后来封了爵，无疑是他那一代最风雅的画家——在才子部落里算得一位相当伶俐的侍臣；正是经这位先生，我认识了一打这路人物和他们的魁首约翰逊先生——雷诺兹替我画过一幅：我、林登夫人和我们的小布莱恩，画展上大受称赞（画上的我穿着蒂普尔顿义勇骑兵队的军装离妻而去——我在队里当少校；孩子一见我的头盔就惊退，活像那位叫什么来着——赫克托耳的儿子，波普先生《伊利亚特》里写的那个）。总之，经雷诺兹先生，我被引见给这班先生，同他们的魁首也搭上了。我一向当他们的魁首是头大狗熊。他在我家喝过两三回茶，失礼得出奇；拿我的见解当学童的见解待，一点也不客气；叫我管好马匹和裁缝，别去沾文学。他那苏格兰的熊倌博斯韦尔先生，是头一流的活宝。他在索霍区卡莱尔宅科尼利夫人的一次舞会上，穿他所谓的“科西嘉装”亮相，那副尊容我从未见过第二副。只是同那家场所有关的故事并非天下最有教益的故事，不然我倒能讲出几十桩那里的古怪行径。全城的高等半上流女子都在那里聚集，从安卡斯特公爵到我的同胞、可怜的诗人奥利弗·哥尔德斯密斯先生，从金斯顿公爵夫人到“天堂鸟”、也就是基蒂·费希尔。我在那里遇见过不少古怪角色，下场也古怪：可怜的哈克曼，后来为杀雷伊小姐上了绞架；还有（悄悄地）尊贵的西蒙尼博士——我的朋友、小剧院的萨姆·富特曾叮嘱他活下来，可伪造罪和绞绳还是早早结果了这位倒霉牧师的前程。
那个年月的伦敦是个快活地方，这是实情。如今我在痛风缠身的暮年执笔，人们比十八世纪末——那时世界同我一样年轻——道德得多、实在得多了。

===Original===

There was a difference between a gentleman and a common fellow in those times. We wore silk and embroidery then. Now every man has the same coachmanlike look in his belcher and caped coat, and there is no outward difference between my Lord and his groom. Then it took a man of fashion a couple of hours to make his toilette, and he could show some taste and genius in the selecting it. What a blaze of splendour was a drawing-room, or an opera, of a gala night! What sums of money were lost and won at the delicious faro-table! My gilt curricle and outriders, blazing in green and gold, were very different objects from the equipages you see nowadays in the ring, with the stunted grooms behind them. A man could drink four times as much as the milksops nowadays can swallow; but ’tis useless expatiating on this theme. Gentlemen are dead and gone. The fashion has now turned upon your soldiers and sailors, and I grow quite moody and sad when I think of thirty years ago.
This is a chapter devoted to reminiscences of what was a very happy and splendid time with me, but presenting little of mark in the way of adventure; as is generally the case when times are happy and easy. It would seem idle to fill pages with accounts of the everyday occupations of a man of fashion﻿—the fair ladies who smiled upon him, the dresses he wore, the matches he played, and won or lost. At this period of time, when youngsters are employed cutting the Frenchmen’s throats in Spain and France, lying out in bivouacs, and feeding off commissariat beef and biscuit, they would not understand what a life their ancestors led; and so I shall leave further discourse upon the pleasures of the times when even the Prince was a lad in leading-strings, when Charles Fox had not subsided into a mere statesman, and Bonaparte was a beggarly brat in his native island.
Whilst these improvements were going on in my estates﻿—my house, from an antique Norman castle, being changed to an elegant Greek temple, or palace﻿—my gardens and woods losing their rustic appearance to be adapted to the most genteel French style﻿—my child growing up at his mother’s knees, and my influence in the country increasing﻿—it must not be imagined that I stayed in Devonshire all this while, and that I neglected to make visits to London, and my various estates in England and Ireland.

===Chinese===

那个年头，绅士同粗人是分得出的。那时我们穿丝绸和绣花。如今人人都裹着大围巾、披肩大衣，一副车夫相，勋爵同他的马夫外表上再无分别。那时一位时兴人梳妆要两个钟头，还梳得出点趣味和天分。御觐夜的宫里、歌剧院里，是何等一片珠光宝气！那迷人的法罗赌桌上，进出的是何等数目！我那辆绿金二色、煊赫一世的轻便双轮车带着前导骑，同如今跑马场圈里你见到的那些车马——后面蹲着矮脚马夫——是两样的物件。那时一个人的酒量顶得上如今的娘娘腔四倍；可在这题目上铺陈无益。绅士们死绝了。如今的风气轮到了你们的水兵陆军人；一想起三十年前，我就闷闷地伤感。
这一章专写回忆——我一生中一段极快活极煊赫的时光，只是论奇遇没多少可记；日子一快活一安逸，向来如此。拿一个时兴人的日常起居——对他微笑的美人们、他穿的衣裳、他打过的牌局输赢——来填纸页，未免无聊。如今这年月，小伙子们正忙着在西班牙和法兰西割法国人的喉咙、露营旷野、啃军需处的牛肉和硬饼，他们不会懂得他们的祖先过的是什么样的日子；所以那个时代的乐事，我也就不再多讲——那时连亲王还在襁袺里牵着手，查尔斯·福克斯还没退化成一个纯粹的政治家，波拿巴还是他本岛上个要饭的小崽子。
我的产业正这么翻新——我的宅子，由一座诺曼古堡改成一座雅致的希腊神庙或宫殿——我的园林脱去村野气，改就最时兴的法国式样——我的孩子在母亲膝下长大，我在乡间的势力日增——可别以为这期间我一直待在德文郡，忘了去伦敦、忘了巡视我在英格兰和爱尔兰的各处产业。

===Original===

I went to reside at the Trecothick estate and the Polwellan Wheal, where I found, instead of profit, every kind of pettifogging chicanery; I passed over in state to our territories in Ireland, where I entertained the gentry in a style the Lord Lieutenant himself could not equal; gave the fashion to Dublin (to be sure it was a beggarly savage city in those days; and, since the time there has been a pother about the Union, and the misfortunes attending it, I have been at a loss to account for the mad praises of the old order of things, which the fond Irish patriots have invented); I say I set the fashion to Dublin; and small praise to me, for a poor place it was in those times, whatever the Irish party may say.
In a former chapter I have given you a description of it. It was the Warsaw of our part of the world: there was a splendid, ruined, half-civilised nobility, ruling over a half-savage population. I say half-savage advisedly. The commonalty in the streets were wild, unshorn, and in rags. The most public places were not safe after nightfall. The College, the public buildings, and the great gentry’s houses were splendid (the latter unfinished for the most part); but the people were in a state more wretched than any vulgar I have ever known: the exercise of their religion was only half allowed to them; their clergy were forced to be educated out of the country; their aristocracy was quite distinct from them; there was a Protestant nobility, and in the towns, poor insolent Protestant corporations, with a bankrupt retinue of mayors, aldermen, and municipal officers﻿—all of whom figured in addresses and had the public voice in the country; but there was no sympathy and connection between the upper and the lower people of the Irish. To one who had been bred so much abroad as myself, this difference between Catholic and Protestant was doubly striking; and though as firm as a rock in my own faith, yet I could not help remembering my grandfather held a different one, and wondering that there should be such a political difference between the two. I passed among my neighbours for a dangerous leveller, for entertaining and expressing such opinions, and especially for asking the priest of the parish to my table at Castle Lyndon.

===Chinese===

我到特雷科西克的产业和波尔韦兰矿住过一阵；在那里我找到的不是进项，而是各式小律师式的刁钻欺诈；我又排场十足地巡视了我们在爱尔兰的领地，在那儿款待乡绅的气派，总督大人本人也比不上；我给都柏林（Dublin）立了风气（说实在的，那时节那是个又穷又野的城；自从合并风波和随之而来的种种不幸闹起来，那些痴心的爱尔兰爱国之士编出对旧秩序的疯狂颂词，我一直纳闷）；我说我给都柏林立了风气；这也值不得夸——那时的都柏林就是个可怜地方，随爱尔兰党怎么说吧。
前头某一章我给过诸位描写。那是我们这半个世界的华沙：一个煊赫、破落、半开化的贵族，统治着一群半野蛮的百姓。我说半野蛮，是斟酌过的。街上的平民蓬头垢面，衣不蔽体。天黑以后，最热闹的去处也不安全。学院、公共建筑和大户人家的宅子倒华丽（后者多半没完工）；可百姓那种穷苦，我见过的贱民里没有比得上的：他们的宗教只被容许信一半；他们的神职人员被迫出国受教；他们的贵族同他们全然两不相干：有的是新教贵族，镇子里又有倨傲的穷新教自治体，带着一班破产的市长、市参事和市政官员的扈从——这些人出尽风头、把持着乡间的舆论；可爱尔兰上下两层人民之间，没有半点同情与联结。在我这样一个久居外国的人看来，天主教同新教的这道鸿沟加倍刺眼；我自己的信仰固然坚如磐石，可我忍不住想起我祖父持的是另一种信仰，纳闷这两者之间何以有这样一道政治的天堑。因为持这样的议论——尤其因为我把本堂神父请上林登堡（Castle Lyndon）的餐桌——左邻右舍都当我是危险的平均派。

===Original===

He was a gentleman, educated at Salamanca, and, to my mind, a far better bred and more agreeable companion than his comrade the rector, who had but a dozen Protestants for his congregation; who was a lord’s son, to be sure, but he could hardly spell, and the great field of his labours was in the kennel and cockpit.
I did not extend and beautify the house of Castle Lyndon as I had done our other estates, but contented myself with paying an occasional visit there; exercising an almost royal hospitality, and keeping open house during my stay. When absent, I gave to my aunt, the widow Brady, and her six unmarried daughters (although they always detested me), permission to inhabit the place; my mother preferring my new mansion of Barryogue.
And as my Lord Bullingdon was by this time grown excessively tall and troublesome, I determined to leave him under the care of a proper governor in Ireland, with Mrs. Brady and her six daughters to take care of him; and he was welcome to fall in love with all the old ladies if he were so minded, and thereby imitate his stepfather’s example. When tired of Castle Lyndon, his Lordship was at liberty to go and reside at my house with my mamma; but there was no love lost between him and her, and, on account of my son Bryan, I think she hated him as cordially as ever I myself could possibly do.
The county of Devon is not so lucky as the neighbouring county of Cornwall, and has not the share of representatives which the latter possesses; where I have known a moderate country gentleman, with a few score of hundreds per annum from his estate, treble his income by returning three or four Members to Parliament, and by the influence with Ministers which these seats gave him. The parliamentary interest of the house of Lyndon had been grossly neglected during my wife’s minority, and the incapacity of the Earl her father; or, to speak more correctly, it had been smuggled away from the Lyndon family altogether by the adroit old hypocrite of Tiptoff Castle, who acted as most kinsmen and guardians do by their wards and relatives, and robbed them. The Marquess of Tiptoff returned four Members to Parliament: two for the borough of Tippleton, which, as all the world knows, lies at the foot of our estate of Hackton, bounded on the other side by Tiptoff Park.

===Chinese===

那位神父是位绅士，萨拉曼卡出身；在我看，比他那位同事、本堂牧师有教养得多、可亲得多——那位牧师的会众拢共一打新教徒；他倒是位勋爵之子，只是几乎不会拼字，劳作的大田在狗窝和斗鸡场。
林登堡我没有像别的产业那样扩充美化，只满足于偶尔去看看；在时行使几乎王家的好客，留住期间门户大开。不住时，我把宅子给我的姨母布雷迪寡妇同她六个未嫁的女儿住（虽然她们一直恨我）；我母亲偏爱我的新宅巴里奥格。
布林登勋爵这时已长得个头奇高、格外难缠，我决意把他留在爱尔兰，交给一位合适的监护人，由布雷迪太太同她六个女儿照看；他若有意，尽管同那几位老小姐谈情说爱，正好学他继父的样。住腻了林登堡，他勋爵尽可去巴里奥格我母亲家住；可他同我母亲也没有情分，并且为着我儿子布莱恩，我看她恨他恨得同我一样切心。
德文郡不如邻郡康沃尔走运，议员名额没有康沃尔多；在康沃尔，我见过一位每年地里收几百镑的寻常乡绅，靠往议会送三四名议员、靠这些席位在阁员那儿挣到的势力，把进项翻了三倍。林登家的议会势力，在我妻子未成年和她父亲无能的年月里，荒废得一塌糊涂；或者说得准些，叫蒂普托夫堡那只老奸巨猾的伪君子整个儿偷走了——他待他的被监护人和亲戚，正同多数监护亲戚一个样：劫。蒂普托夫侯爵往议会里送四名议员：两名替蒂普尔顿镇——那镇子尽人皆知，就卧在我们哈克顿产业的脚下，另一边以蒂普托夫园为界。

===Original===

For time out of mind we had sent Members for that borough, until Tiptoff, taking advantage of the late lord’s imbecility, put in his own nominees. When his eldest son became of age, of course my Lord was to take his seat for Tippleton; when Rigby (Nabob Rigby, who made his fortune under Clive in India) died, the Marquess thought fit to bring down his second son, my Lord George Poynings, to whom I have introduced the reader in a former chapter, and determined, in his high mightiness, that he too should go in and swell the ranks of the Opposition﻿—the big old Whigs, with whom the Marquess acted.
Rigby had been for some time in an ailing condition previous to his demise, and you may be sure that the circumstance of his failing health had not been passed over by the gentry of the county, who were staunch Government men for the most part, and hated my Lord Tiptoff’s principles as dangerous and ruinous. “We have been looking out for a man to fight against him,” said the squires to me; “we can only match Tiptoff out of Hackton Castle. You, Mr. Lyndon, are our man, and at the next county election we will swear to bring you in.”
I hated the Tiptoffs so, that I would have fought them at any election. They not only would not visit at Hackton, but declined to receive those who visited us; they kept the women of the county from receiving my wife: they invented half the wild stories of my profligacy and extravagance with which the neighbourhood was entertained; they said I had frightened my wife into marriage, and that she was a lost woman; they hinted that Bullingdon’s life was not secure under my roof, that his treatment was odious, and that I wanted to put him out of the way to make place for Bryan my son. I could scarce have a friend to Hackton, but they counted the bottles drunk at my table. They ferreted out my dealings with my lawyers and agents. If a creditor was unpaid, every item of his bill was known at Tiptoff Hall; if I looked at a farmer’s daughter, it was said I had ruined her. My faults are many, I confess, and as a domestic character, I can’t boast of any particular regularity or temper; but Lady Lyndon and I did not quarrel more than fashionable people do, and, at first, we always used to make it up pretty well. I am a man full of errors, certainly, but not the devil that these odious backbiters at Tiptoff represented me to be.

===Chinese===

自古以来，那镇子的议员都由我们家送，直到蒂普托夫趁老勋爵不济，塞进了他自己的人。他长子一到岁数，自然由勋爵大人坐蒂普尔顿的席；里格比（纳博布·里格比，在印度跟克莱夫发的家）一死，侯爵认为该把次子乔治·波因宁斯勋爵请出来——前头一章我已向读者引见过他——并且凭他的赫赫威权，打定主意也叫他进去，给反对派的队伍添数——那伙老辉格巨擘，侯爵就是同他们一路的。
里格比死前已缠绵病榻多时；诸位想得到，他病势日沉这件事，瞒不过本郡的乡绅——他们多半是死心塌地的政府派，恨蒂普托夫勋爵的原则，当它危险、当它祸国。“我们早物色人同他斗了，”乡绅们对我说，“斗蒂普托夫，只有哈克顿堡出得来人。您，林登先生，就是我们的人；下回郡选，我们保您进。”
我恨蒂普托夫一家恨到这地步，任何选举我都肯同他们斗。他们不但不来哈克顿做客，连来做我们客的也不肯接待；他们拦着全郡的太太们不接待我夫人；我在邻里间流传的那些放荡挥霍的浑话，一多半是他们编的；他们说我把夫人吓进了婚姻，说她是个毁了的女人；他们放风说布林登在我屋檐下性命不保、待他可恶至极、说我要除了他给儿子布莱恩腾地方。我在哈克顿简直没法有朋友，可他们连我桌上喝掉的酒瓶都数着。他们刺探我同律师、代理人的往来。一个债主没拿到钱，他账上的每一项蒂普托夫府全知道；我多看了哪家农户姑娘一眼，就传说我毁了她。我的毛病很多，我认；治家一节，我夸不得什么规律和脾气；可林登夫人和我拌嘴，并不比时兴人家拌得多，而且起初总回回和得漂亮。我是个一身过错的人，这是实情；可绝不是蒂普托夫那伙可憎的进谗者描画出的魔鬼。

===Original===

For the first three years I never struck my wife but when I was in liquor. When I flung the carving-knife at Bullingdon I was drunk, as everybody present can testify; but as for having any systematic scheme against the poor lad, I can declare solemnly that, beyond merely hating him (and one’s inclinations are not in one’s power), I am guilty of no evil towards him.
I had sufficient motives, then, for enmity against the Tiptoffs, and am not a man to let a feeling of that kind lie inactive. Though a Whig, or, perhaps, because a Whig, the Marquess was one of the haughtiest men breathing, and treated commoners as his idol the great Earl used to treat them﻿—after he came to a coronet himself﻿—as so many low vassals, who might be proud to lick his shoe-buckle. When the Tippleton mayor and corporation waited upon him, he received them covered, never offered Mr. Mayor a chair, but retired when the refreshments were brought, or had them served to the worshipful aldermen in the steward’s room. These honest Britons never rebelled against such treatment, until instructed to do so by my patriotism. No, the dogs liked to be bullied; and, in the course of a long experience, I have met with but very few Englishmen who are not of their way of thinking.
It was not until I opened their eyes that they knew their degradation. I invited the Mayor to Hackton, and Mrs. Mayoress (a very buxom pretty groceress she was, by the way) I made sit by my wife, and drove them both out to the races in my curricle. Lady Lyndon fought very hard against this condescension; but I had a way with her, as the saying is, and though she had a temper, yet I had a better one. A temper, psha! A wildcat has a temper, but a keeper can get the better of it; and I know very few women in the world whom I could not master.

===Chinese===

头三年，我只在喝醉时才动手打老婆。我朝布林登掠切肉刀那回，我是醉了，在场人人可证；至于说对那可怜孩子有什么一套一套的算计，我可以庄严声明：除了单纯恨他（人的好恶由不得自己），我于他无罪。
这么说，我对蒂普托夫家的敌意理由十足；我也不是把这类感情搁着不动的人。这位侯爵虽是辉格——或者正因为是辉格——是天下最高傲的人物之一，待平民同他那位偶像大伯爵自己戴上冠冕以后一样——只当是一群低贱的佃奴，舔他的鞋扣都该感恩。蒂普尔顿市长率自治体晋见，他戴着帽子接见，市长先生赏不赏座一个没有，茶点一上来他便退席，或者吩咐把茶点送到管家房里去孝敬各位参事老爷。这批老实的英国人从不曾反抗这样的对待，直到我的爱国心开导了他们。不，这班狗就爱受欺；以我漫长的阅历，英国人不作此想的，我遇过的寥寥无几。
我开了他们的眼，他们才知自己的下贱。我请市长来哈克顿，请市长夫人（顺便说一句，是位丰润标致的杂货铺老板娘）坐在我夫人身边，用我的双轮车带他们两位去看赛马。林登夫人同这番屈尊缠斗得很凶；可我对她自有一手，俗话说；她有脾气，我的脾气更好。脾气，呸！野猫也有脾气，可看兽人制服得了；世上我制服不了的女人，我知道的没有几个。

===Original===

Well, I made much of the mayor and corporation; sent them bucks for their dinners, or asked them to mine; made a point of attending their assemblies, dancing with their wives and daughters, going through, in short, all the acts of politeness which are necessary on such occasions: and though old Tiptoff must have seen my goings on, yet his head was so much in the clouds, that he never once condescended to imagine his dynasty could be overthrown in his own town of Tippleton, and issued his mandates as securely as if he had been the Grand Turk, and the Tippletonians no better than so many slaves of his will.
Every post which brought us any account of Rigby’s increasing illness, was the sure occasion of a dinner from me; so much so, that my friends of the hunt used to laugh and say, “Rigby’s worse; there’s a corporation dinner at Hackton.”
It was in 1776, when the American war broke out, that I came into Parliament. My Lord Chatham, whose wisdom his party in those days used to call superhuman, raised his oracular voice in the House of Peers against the American contest; and my countryman, Mr. Burke﻿—a great philosopher, but a plaguy long-winded orator﻿—was the champion of the rebels in the Commons﻿—where, however, thanks to British patriotism, he could get very few to back him. Old Tiptoff would have sworn black was white if the great Earl had bidden him; and he made his son give up his commission in the Guards, in imitation of my Lord Pitt, who resigned his ensigncy rather than fight against what he called his American brethren.
But this was a height of patriotism extremely little relished in England, where, ever since the breaking out of hostilities, our people hated the Americans heartily; and where, when we heard of the fight of Lexington, and the glorious victory of Bunker’s Hill (as we used to call it in those days), the nation flushed out in its usual hotheaded anger. The talk was all against the philosophers after that, and the people were most indomitably loyal. It was not until the land-tax was increased, that the gentry began to grumble a little; but still my party in the West was very strong against the Tiptoffs, and I determined to take the field and win as usual.
The old Marquess neglected every one of the decent precautions which are requisite in a parliamentary campaign.

===Chinese===

总之，我把市长和自治体捧得高高儿的：给他们饭桌送鹿肉，或请他们上我的桌；他们的舞会我必定到场，同他们的太太女儿跳舞——总之，凡此等场合应有的礼数，一样不缺。老蒂普托夫纵然看出了我的动静，无奈他脑袋悬在云端，从没肯屈尊想一想，他自家的蒂普尔顿镇里，他的王朝也可能被掀翻；他发号施令，安然得同大土耳其苏丹一样，只当蒂普尔顿人全是听他使唤的奴隶。
邮路每带来一分里格比病重的消息，必是我设宴的日子；以至猎会的朋友们惯常笑着说：“里格比又重了；哈克顿有自治体的席了。”
1776年，美洲战事爆发那一年，我进了议会。查塔姆勋爵——他那一党当年称他的智慧超凡入圣——在上院扬起他神谕般的嗓子，反对美洲之战；我的同胞伯克先生——大哲学家，可惜天生一副臭长的气——在下院做了叛党的旗手，不过，托英国爱国心的福，肯附和他的寥寥。老蒂普托夫，大伯爵若吩咐他黑的是白，他也肯赌咒；他叫儿子辞了近卫军的军职，学皮特勋爵的样——皮特宁可辞掉旗官衔，也不肯同他所谓的美洲同胞交战。
可这一种爱国的高调，在英国极不受用；开战以来，我国百姓恨美洲人恨得咬牙；列克星敦接仗、邦克山“光荣大捷”（那时我们这样叫它）的消息传来，举国照例烧起了那股急火。那以后舆论全在哲学家们的对面，百姓的忠君热谁也压不倒。直到土地税加了，乡绅们才咕哝起来；可我在西部反蒂普托夫的势力仍然极强，我决意上阵，照例去赢。
老侯爵把议会选战该有的一切像样的防范，一样也没放在心上。

===Original===

He signified to the corporation and freeholders his intention of presenting his son, Lord George, and his desire that the latter should be elected their burgess; but he scarcely gave so much as a glass of beer to whet the devotedness of his adherents: and I, as I need not say, engaged every tavern in Tippleton in my behalf.
There is no need to go over the twenty-times-told tale of an election. I rescued the borough of Tippleton from the hands of Lord Tiptoff and his son, Lord George. I had a savage sort of satisfaction, too, in forcing my wife (who had been at one time exceedingly smitten by her kinsman, as I have already related) to take part against him, and to wear and distribute my colours when the day of election came. And when we spoke at one another, I told the crowd that I had beaten Lord George in love, that I had beaten him in war, and that I would now beat him in Parliament; and so I did, as the event proved: for, to the inexpressible anger of the old Marquess, Barry Lyndon, Esquire, was returned member of Parliament for Tippleton, in place of John Rigby, Esquire, deceased; and I threatened him at the next election to turn him out of *both* his seats, and went to attend my duties in Parliament.
It was then I seriously determined on achieving for myself the Irish peerage, to be enjoyed after me by my beloved son and heir.

===Chinese===

他知会自治体和自由持有人们：他有意叫他儿子乔治勋爵出头，愿众人选他做代表；可他连一杯啤酒也不大肯破费，去润一润党羽的热忱；而我呢——不必说——把蒂普尔顿的每家酒馆都替我包了下来。
选举那套讲了二十遍的老故事，不必再讲一遍。我把蒂普尔顿镇从蒂普托夫勋爵和他儿子乔治勋爵手里夺了过来。我还有一股野路子的快意：逼我夫人（她一度深深迷恋她那位表亲，前文已叙）站到他的对面，到投票之日，佩我的彩章、散发我的彩章。我们在台上对讲时，我告诉民众：情场上我打败过乔治勋爵，战场上我打败过他，如今在议会里我也要打败他；事实也果然如此：老侯爵气得没法说，巴里·林登绅士当选蒂普尔顿镇议员，接替故约翰·里格比绅士；我还掠下话，下回选举要把他两个席位都掀掉；然后我上伦敦，赴我的议会之职去了。
正是那时，我郑重拿定主意，要给自己挣一个爱尔兰贵族的爵位，身后由我心爱的儿子和继承人承享。

## IX I Appear in a Manner Becoming My Name and Lineage / 我以不愧名姓与家世的方式登场
===Original===

Fortune smiling at parting upon Monsieur de Balibari, enabled him to win a handsome sum with his faro-bank.
At ten o’clock the next morning, the carriage of the Chevalier de Balibari drew up as usual at the door of his hotel; and the Chevalier, who was at his window, seeing the chariot arrive, came down the stairs in his usual stately manner.
“Where is my rascal Ambrose?” said he, looking around and not finding his servant to open the door.
“I will let down the steps for your honour,” said a gendarme, who was standing by the carriage; and no sooner had the Chevalier entered, than the officer jumped in after him, another mounted the box by the coachman, and the latter began to drive.
“Good gracious!” said the Chevalier, “what is this?”
“You are going to drive to the frontier,” said the gendarme, touching his hat.
“It is shameful﻿—infamous! I insist upon being put down at the Austrian Ambassador’s house!”
“I have orders to gag your honour if you cry out,” said the gendarme.
“All Europe shall hear of this!” said the Chevalier, in a fury.
“As you please,” answered the officer, and then both relapsed into silence.
The silence was not broken between Berlin and Potsdam, through which place the Chevalier passed as His Majesty was reviewing his guards there, and the regiments of Bülow, Zitwitz, and Henkel de Donnersmark. As the Chevalier passed His Majesty, the King raised his hat and said, “*Qu’il ne descende pas: je lui souhaite un bon voyage.*” The Chevalier de Balibari acknowledged this courtesy by a profound bow.
They had not got far beyond Potsdam, when boom! the alarm cannon began to roar.
“It is a deserter,” said the officer.
“Is it possible?” said the Chevalier, and sank back into his carriage again.
Hearing the sound of the guns, the common people came out along the road with fowling-pieces and pitchforks, in hopes to catch the truant. The gendarmes seemed very anxious to be on the lookout for him too. The price of a deserter was fifty crowns to those who brought him in.
“Confess, sir,” said the Chevalier to the police officer in the carriage with him, “that you long to be rid of me, from whom you can get nothing, and to be on the lookout for the deserter who may bring you in fifty crowns? Why not tell the postilion to push on? You may land me at the frontier and get back to your hunt all the sooner.”

===Chinese===

命运女神临别时对巴利巴里先生嫣然一笑，让他的法罗赌台赢进了一笔可观的钱财。
第二天上午10点，巴利巴里骑士的马车照例停到旅馆门前；骑士正倚窗闲坐，望见车到，便以他惯常的雍容气派踱下楼梯来。
“我那个泼皮安布罗斯哪儿去了？”他说，四下张望，不见他的仆人来开车门。
“小的来伺候大人放踏板，”守在车旁的一名宪兵说；骑士前脚跨进车厢，那军官后脚便跟着跳了进来，另一个蹬上了车夫旁的驭座，马车随即启动。
“天哪！”骑士说，“这是唱的哪一出？”
“大人是被送往边境去，”宪兵碰了碰帽檐说。
“无耻——卑鄙！我要求立刻在奥地利大使公馆门前停车！”
“大人要是喊叫，小的奉命堵嘴，”宪兵说。
“全欧洲都会知道这桩事！”骑士勃然大怒。
“悉听尊便，”军官答道，随后两人重又陷入沉默。
从柏林到波茨坦，这沉默一直没有打破。骑士途经波茨坦时，国王陛下正在那里检阅他的近卫军，还有比洛、齐特维茨和亨克尔·冯·唐纳斯马克的几个团。骑士驶过御驾之前，国王举了举帽子，说道：“别叫他下车：祝他一路顺风。”巴利巴里骑士深深一躬，领受这份礼数。
出了波茨坦没多远，轰隆！警报炮吼了起来。
“是逃兵，”军官说。
“竟有这等事？”骑士说着，又颓然靠回车厢里。
听见炮声，小民百姓纷纷提着鸟枪、扛着草叉沿路而出，指望拿住那逃亡的家伙。宪兵们似乎也眼巴巴地缉拿他。解送一个逃兵归案的赏格，是50克朗。
“承认吧，先生，”骑士对同车的警官说，“您巴不得甩脱我这个榨不出油水的人，好去逮那能给您挣50克朗的逃兵，是不是？何不叫马夫赶快点？把我撂在边境上，您也好早点回头干您的差事。

===Original===

The officer told the postillion to get on; but the way seemed intolerably long to the Chevalier. Once or twice he thought he heard the noise of horse galloping behind: his own horses did not seem to go two miles an hour; but they *did* go. The black and white barriers came in view at last, hard by Bruck, and opposite them the green and yellow of Saxony. The Saxon customhouse officers came out.
“I have no luggage,” said the Chevalier.
“The gentleman has nothing contraband,” said the Prussian officers, grinning, and took their leave of their prisoner with much respect.
The Chevalier de Balibari gave them a Frederic apiece.
“Gentlemen,” said he, “I wish you a good day. Will you please to go to the house whence we set out this morning, and tell my man there to send on my baggage to the Three Kings at Dresden?”
Then ordering fresh horses, the Chevalier set off on his journey for that capital. I need not tell you that *I* was the Chevalier.

===Chinese===

军官吩咐马夫加鞭；可在骑士看来，这条路长得难熬。有一两回，他仿佛听见身后马蹄疾驰之声：自家的马似乎一个钟头走不上两英里；可它们毕竟*是*在走。黑白两色的界栅终于望见了，就在布鲁克近旁，对面便是萨克森的绿黄两色。萨克森的关务官员迎了出来。
“我没有行李，”骑士说。
“这位先生身上没有违禁的东西，”普鲁士军官们咧嘴笑道，随即毕恭毕敬地同他们的囚徒作别。
巴利巴里骑士赏了他们每人一枚腓特烈金币。
“诸位先生，”他说，“祝你们日安。劳驾回我们今早动身的那家宅子一趟，吩咐我那仆人把我的行李随后发到德累斯顿的三王旅馆去。”
随后换过新马，骑士登上了赴那座京城的旅途。不必我说，那位骑士就是*我*。

===Original===

> “**Nephew Redmond**﻿—This comes to you by a sure hand, no other than Mr. Lumpit of the English Mission, who is acquainted, as all Berlin will be directly, with our wonderful story. They only know half as yet; they only know that a deserter went off in my clothes, and all are in admiration of your cleverness and valour.
> “I confess that for two hours after your departure I lay in bed in no small trepidation, thinking whether His Majesty might have a fancy to send me to Spandau, for the freak of which we had both been guilty. But in that case I had taken my precautions: I had written a statement of the case to my chief, the Austrian Minister, with the full and true story how you had been set to spy upon me, how you turned out to be my very near relative, how you had been kidnapped yourself into the service, and how we both had determined to effect your escape. The laugh would have been so much against the King, that he never would have dared to lay a finger upon me. What would Monsieur de Voltaire have said to such an act of tyranny? But it was a lucky day, and everything has turned out to my wish. As I lay in my bed two and a half hours after your departure, in comes your ex-Captain Potzdorff. ‘Redmont!’ says he, in his imperious High-Dutch way, ‘are you there?’ No answer. ‘The rogue is gone out,’ said he; and straightway makes for my red box where I keep my love-letters, my glass eye which I used to wear, my favourite lucky dice with which I threw the thirteen mains at Prague; my two sets of Paris teeth, and my other private matters that you know of.
> “He first tried a bunch of keys, but none of them would fit the little English lock. Then my gentleman takes out of his pocket a chisel and hammer, and falls to work like a professional burglar, actually bursting open my little box!
> “Now was my time to act. I advance towards him armed with an immense water-jug. I come noiselessly up to him just as he had broken the box, and with all my might I deal him such a blow over the head as smashes the water-jug to atoms, and sends my captain with a snort lifeless to the ground. I thought I had killed him.
> “Then I ring all the bells in the house; and shout and swear and scream, ‘Thieves!﻿—thieves!﻿—landlord!﻿—murder!﻿—fire!’ until the whole household come tumbling up the stairs. ‘Where is my servant?’ roar I. ‘Who dares to rob me in open day?

===Chinese===

> “**侄儿雷德蒙**——此信托可靠之手带给你，不是别人，正是英国使团的伦皮特先生；我们这段奇遇他已尽知——全柏林转眼也都会知道。眼下他们只知道一半；只知道有个逃兵穿着我的衣裳跑了，人人都叹服你的机敏和胆量。
> 老实招认，你走后有整整两个钟头，我躺在床上心惊胆战，琢磨陛下会不会一时兴起，为我们两人都干下的那桩顽劣把戏，把我送去施潘道。不过真到那步田地，我也早有防备：我已把事情写成一份陈情书，送呈我的上司奥地利公使——你如何奉命来暗访我，如何查明竟是我的至亲，如何自己遭人绑架充了军，你我二人又如何定计放你脱身，原原本本，一一叙明。真闹起来，笑柄尽在国王身上，他断然不敢动我一根毫毛。行出这等暴虐之事，德·伏尔泰先生会怎么说呢？可那天是个吉利日子，事事都如了我的愿。你动身两个半钟头后，我正躺在床上，你那位旧日上司波茨多夫上尉进来了。‘雷德蒙！’他操着那副专横的德国腔喝道，‘在不在？’无人应声。‘这流氓出去了，’他说；随即直奔我的红匣子——那里面收着我的情书、我从前戴的玻璃假眼、我心爱的吉利骰子——在布拉格我靠它掷出过13局好彩——我的两副巴黎假牙，还有你知道的那些私密物件。
> 他先试了一串钥匙，可没有一把对得上那把小小的英国锁。于是这位爷从口袋里掏出凿子和锤子，像个惯窃行家似的干起活来，硬是把我的小匣子撬开了！
> 这正是我动手的良机。我操起一只大水罐向他进击。趁他刚撬开匣子，我悄没声儿挨到近旁，抡圆了朝他天灵盖一罐砸下：水罐砸得粉碎，我的上尉哼出一声，直挺挺栽倒在地。我只道自己送了他的命。
> 接着我把满宅的铃铛摇了个遍；又叫骂、赌咒、尖喊，“有贼！——有贼！——掌柜的！——杀人啦！——着火啦！”直闹得合宅人等跌跌撞撞滚上楼来。‘我的仆人呢？’我吼道。‘光天化日之下，谁敢抢劫我？

===Original===

Look at the villain whom I find in the act of breaking my chest open! Send for the police, send for his Excellency the Austrian Minister! all Europe shall know of this insult!’
> “ ‘Dear Heaven!’ says the landlord, ‘we saw you go away three hours ago!’
> “ ‘*Me!*’ says I; ‘why, man, I have been in bed all the morning. I am ill﻿—I have taken physic﻿—I have not left the house this morning! Where is that scoundrel Ambrose? But, stop! where are my clothes and wig?’ for I was standing before them in my chamber-gown and stockings, with my nightcap on.
> “ ‘I have it﻿—I have it!’ says a little chambermaid: ‘Ambrose is off in your honour’s dress.’
> “ ‘And my money﻿—my money!’ says I; ‘where is my purse with forty-eight Frederics in it? But we have one of the villains left. Officers, seize him!’
> “ ‘It’s the young Herr von Potzdorff!’ says the landlord, more and more astonished.
> “ ‘What! a gentleman breaking open my trunk with hammer and chisel﻿—impossible!’
> “Herr von Potzdorff was returning to life by this time, with a swelling on his skull as big as a saucepan; and the officers carried him off, and the judge who was sent for dressed a procès-verbal of the matter, and I demanded a copy of it, which I sent forthwith to my ambassador.
> “I was kept a prisoner to my room the next day, and a judge, a general, and a host of lawyers, officers, and officials, were set upon me to bully, perplex, threaten, and cajole me. I said it was true you had told me that you had been kidnapped into the service, that I thought you were released from it, and that I had you with the best recommendations. I appealed to my Minister, who was bound to come to my aid; and, to make a long story short, poor Potzdorff is now on his way to Spandau; and his uncle, the elder Potzdorff, has brought me five hundred louis, with a humble request that I would leave Berlin forthwith, and hush up this painful matter.
> “I shall be with you at the Three Crowns the day after you receive this. Ask Mr. Lumpit to dinner. Do not spare your money﻿—you are my son. Everybody in Dresden knows your loving uncle,
> “The Chevalier De Balibari.”

===Chinese===

瞧瞧这个恶棍，竟当着我的面撬我的箱子！快去叫警察，快去请奥地利公使大人！全欧洲都要知道这桩欺辱！’”
> “‘天哪！’掌柜的说，‘3个钟头前我们明明眼看着您走的呀！’
> “‘*我*！’我说；‘嘿，伙计，我一上午都躺在床上呢。我病了——我服了泻药——今儿个根本没出过门！那个恶棍安布罗斯上哪儿去了？慢着！我的衣裳和假发哪儿去了？’——原来我正穿着晨衣和长袜、头上扣着睡帽站在他们面前。
> “‘我知道了——我知道了！’一个小侍女说，‘安布罗斯穿着大人的衣裳跑啦。’
> “‘还有我的钱——我的钱！’我说；‘我那只装着48枚腓特烈金币的钱袋哪儿去了？好在还有一个恶棍没跑脱。军警们，给我拿下！’
> “‘这是年轻的冯·波茨多夫先生呀！’掌柜的越发惊愕了。
> “‘什么！一位绅士拿锤子凿子撬我的箱子——不可能！’
> “冯·波茨多夫先生这时已还过魂来，脑壳上肿起一个平底锅大的疙瘩；军警们把他架走了；请来的法官就本案拟了一份笔录，我索要了一份抄件，即刻送呈了我们的大使。
> “第二天我被软禁在房里，一位法官、一位将军，外加一大群律师、军官、官员轮番向我进攻，威吓的威吓，纠缠的纠缠，恐吓的恐吓，哄骗的哄骗。我说，你确实告诉过我你是被绑架充军的，我当你早已开释，这才凭最好的保荐收留了你。我诉诸我的公使，他理当出面护我；长话短说，可怜的波茨多夫如今正在押赴施潘道的路上；他的伯父老波茨多夫给我送来500路易，低声下气地求我即刻离开柏林，把这桩痛心的事体捂过去。
> “你收到此信的后日，我便到三王旅馆与你相会。请伦皮特先生来吃顿饭。钱不必省着花——你就是我的儿子。德累斯顿人人都识得你亲爱的叔叔，
> “巴利巴里骑士。”

===Original===

And by these wonderful circumstances I was once more free again: and I kept my resolution then made, never to fall more into the hands of any recruiter, and henceforth and forever to be a gentleman.
With this sum of money, and a good run of luck which ensued presently, we were enabled to make no ungenteel figure. My uncle speedily joined me at the inn at Dresden, where, under pretence of illness, I had kept quiet until his arrival; and, as the Chevalier de Balibari was in particular good odour at the Court of Dresden (having been an intimate acquaintance of the late monarch, the Elector, King of Poland, the most dissolute and agreeable of European princes), I was speedily in the very best society of the Saxon capital: where I may say that my own person and manners, and the singularity of the adventures in which I had been a hero, made me especially welcome. There was not a party of the nobility to which the two gentlemen of Balibari were not invited. I had the honour of kissing hands and being graciously received at Court by the Elector, and I wrote home to my mother such a flaming description of my prosperity, that the good soul very nearly forgot her celestial welfare and her confessor, the Reverend Joshua Jowls, in order to come after me to Germany; but travelling was very difficult in those days, and so we were spared the arrival of the good lady.
I think the soul of Harry Barry, my father, who was always so genteel in his turn of mind, must have rejoiced to see the position which I now occupied; all the women anxious to receive me, all the men in a fury; hobnobbing with dukes and counts at supper, dancing minuets with high-well-born baronesses (as they absurdly call themselves in Germany), with lovely excellencies, nay, with highnesses and transparencies themselves: who could compete with the gallant young Irish noble? who would suppose that seven weeks before I had been a common﻿—bah! I am ashamed to think of it! One of the pleasantest moments of my life was at a grand gala at the Electoral Palace, where I had the honour of walking a polonaise with no other than the Margravine of Bayreuth, old Fritz’s own sister: old Fritz’s, whose hateful blue-baize livery I had worn, whose belts I had pipeclayed, and whose abominable rations of small beer and sauerkraut I had swallowed for five years.

===Chinese===

靠着这番奇妙的际遇，我重又做回了自由之身；我也恪守了当时立下的决心：再不落进任何募兵贩子之手，从此以至永远，做一个上等人。
有这一笔钱财，加上随后接上一阵好手气，我们过出来的排场绝不算寒酸。叔叔很快到德累斯顿的旅馆来同我会合——我在那里托病静养，一直安分待到他来。巴利巴里骑士在德累斯顿宫廷里圣眷尤隆（他是先君、波兰国王兼选帝侯的至交；欧洲的君侯里头，数那位君主最风流放荡，也数他最讨人喜欢），于是我很快便打进了萨克森京城最上等的圈子：可以说，凭我本人的人品仪度，加上我充当主角的那段奇遇之奇，我处处大受欢迎。贵族人家开堂会，没有不请我们巴利巴里两位绅士的。我有幸在宫中觐见选帝侯，吻手承恩；我又给母亲大人写家信，把我一时的兴旺吹得天花乱坠，害得那位好人大人险些撇下她灵魂的福祉和她的忏悔神师乔舒亚·乔尔斯牧师，千里迢迢赶到德国来寻我；幸亏那年头出门旅行万般艰难，我们才算免了接待这位老太太的一番操劳。
我想，我父亲哈里·巴里的在天之灵——他老人家心思向来讲究体面——见了我如今的地位，必定欢欣鼓舞：女眷们个个巴望接待我，男人们个个妒火中烧；夜宵桌上与公爵伯爵们把盏，同“出身高贵无比”的男爵夫人们（德国人可笑的自称）跳小步舞，同可爱的女“阁下”们跳，甚至同“殿下”们、“透亮贵人”们本人跳：谁能同这位年少风流的爱尔兰贵族争锋？谁想得到7个星期之前，我还是个普通大兵——呸！想起来都臊得慌！我平生最得意的时刻之一，是在选帝侯宫里的一场盛大会典上，我有幸请到拜罗伊特藩侯夫人——老弗里茨的亲妹妹——同行一曲波兰舞：正是老弗里茨，他那可憎的蓝呢号衣我穿过，他的皮带我用白垩擦过，他那可恶的小啤酒和酸白菜配给，我足足吞咽了5年。

===Original===

Having won an English chariot from an Italian gentleman at play, my uncle had our arms painted on the panels in a more splendid way than ever, surmounted (as we were descended from the ancient kings) with an Irish crown of the most splendid size and gilding. I had this crown in lieu of a coronet engraved on a large amethyst signet-ring worn on my forefinger; and I don’t mind confessing that I used to say the jewel had been in my family for several thousand years, having originally belonged to my direct ancestor, his late Majesty King Brian Boru, or Barry. I warrant the legends of the Heralds’ College are not more authentic than mine was.
At first the Minister and the gentlemen at the English hotel used to be rather shy of us two Irish noblemen, and questioned our pretensions to rank. The Minister was a lord’s son, it is true, but he was likewise a grocer’s grandson; and so I told him at Count Lobkowitz’s masquerade. My uncle, like a noble gentleman as he was, knew the pedigree of every considerable family in Europe. He said it was the only knowledge befitting a gentleman; and when we were not at cards, we would pass hours over Gwillim or D’Hozier, reading the genealogies, learning the blazons, and making ourselves acquainted with the relationships of our class. Alas! the noble science is going into disrepute now: so are cards, without which studies and pastimes I can hardly conceive how a man of honour can exist.
My first affair of honour with a man of undoubted fashion was on the score of my nobility, with young Sir Rumford Bumford of the English embassy; my uncle at the same time sending a cartel to the Minister, who declined to come. I shot Sir Rumford in the leg, amidst the tears of joy of my uncle, who accompanied me to the ground; and I promise you that none of the young gentlemen questioned the authenticity of my pedigree, or laughed at my Irish crown again.
What a delightful life did we now lead! I knew I was born a gentleman, from the kindly way in which I took to the business: as business it certainly is. For though it *seems* all pleasure, yet I assure any lowbred persons who may chance to read this, that we, their betters, have to work as well as they: though I did not rise until noon, yet had I not been up at play until long past midnight? Many a time have we come home to bed as the troops were marching out to early parade; and oh!

===Chinese===

叔叔在牌桌上从一位意大利绅士手里赢来一辆英国驷车，便把我们的纹章画上车门板，画得比先前更见华丽，纹章顶上（既然我们是古代列王的苗裔）还加了一顶爱尔兰王冠，尺寸之大、镀金之亮，冠绝当世。我把这顶王冠——权充我家的宝冠——刻在一枚硕大的紫水晶图章戒指上，戴在食指；我也不怕招认：我一向对外人说，这件珍宝在我家传了几千年，最早属于我的直系祖先、已故的布赖恩·博鲁国王陛下，又称巴里王。我敢担保，纹章院那套谱牒传奇，也不见得比我的更可信。
起初，公使大人同英国旅馆里的绅士们，对我们这两位爱尔兰贵族未免存着戒心，怀疑我们的门第成色。公使诚然是勋爵之子，可他也是杂货商之孙；在洛布科维茨伯爵家的化装舞会上，我便这样当面回敬了他。我叔叔不愧是位贵胄绅士，欧洲稍有名气的人家，家谱他无不了然。他说，这是一门绅士唯一配修习的学问；每逢不上牌桌，我们便对着格威利姆或多齐耶的谱牒消磨几个时辰：读世系，记纹章，把咱们这一阶层的姻亲党援摸得滚瓜烂熟。可叹啊！这门高贵的学问如今声名扫地了：牌也一同声名扫地——离了这两样功课与消遣，我真设想不出一个荣誉之士如何活得下去。
我头一桩同毫无争议的时髦人物之间的荣誉公案，就是为着我的贵族名分起的，对头是英国使馆的年轻绅士拉姆福德·班福德爵士；叔叔同时给公使也递了战书，那位却辞谢不至。在陪赴场的叔叔的喜泪涟涟之中，我一枪打中了拉姆福德爵士的腿；我担保，此后那班年轻绅士再没有谁疑心我谱系的真实，也没人敢再取笑我那顶爱尔兰王冠了。
我们如今过的这是何等殹意的日子！我天生便是绅士，这从我上手这门营生时的那份亲切顺遂便看得出：它确确实实是一门营生。别看它*仿佛*全是行乐，我倒要奉告哪位碰巧读到此处的粗人：我们这些他们的上等人，同他们一样也得当差：我虽说晌午才起身，可难道不是后半夜还在牌桌上熬着？多少回，大军开出去出早操，我们才摸回家睡觉；哦！

===Original===

it did my heart good to hear the bugles blowing the reveille before daybreak, or to see the regiments marching out to exercise, and think that I was no longer bound to that disgusting discipline, but restored to my natural station.
I came into it at once, and as if I had never done anything else all my life. I had a gentleman to wait upon me, a French friseur to dress my hair of a morning; I knew the taste of chocolate as by intuition almost, and could distinguish between the right Spanish and the French before I had been a week in my new position; I had rings on all my fingers, watches in both my fobs, canes, trinkets, and snuffboxes of all sorts, and each outvying the other in elegance. I had the finest natural taste for lace and china of any man I ever knew; I could judge a horse as well as any Jew dealer in Germany; in shooting and athletic exercises I was unrivalled; I could not spell, but I could speak German and French cleverly. I had at the least twelve suits of clothes; three richly embroidered with gold, two laced with silver, a garnet-coloured velvet pelisse lined with sable; one of French grey, silver-laced, and lined with chinchilla. I had damask morning robes. I took lessons on the guitar, and sang French catches exquisitely. Where, in fact, was there a more accomplished gentleman than Redmond de Balibari?
All the luxuries becoming my station could not, of course, be purchased without credit and money: to procure which, as our patrimony had been wasted by our ancestors, and we were above the vulgarity and slow returns and doubtful chances of trade, my uncle kept a faro-bank. We were in partnership with a Florentine, well known in all the Courts of Europe, the Count Alessandro Pippi, as skilful a player as ever was seen; but he turned out a sad knave latterly, and I have discovered that his countship was a mere imposture. My uncle was maimed, as I have said; Pippi, like all impostors, was a coward; it was my unrivalled skill with the sword, and readiness to use it, that maintained the reputation of the firm, so to speak, and silenced many a timid gambler who might have hesitated to pay his losings. We always played on parole with anybody: any person, that is, of honour and noble lineage. We never pressed for our winnings or declined to receive promissory notes in lieu of gold. But woe to the man who did not pay when the note became due!

===Chinese===

天亮前听号角吹起床号，看各团开出去操练，想到自己再不受那套讨厌的军纪约束、已归位到生来属于我的品级，心里真是说不出的熨帖。
我一脚踏进这门生活，便仿佛生平没干过别的。有位绅士贴身伺候，每晨有法国理发师替我做头发；巧克力的滋味，我几乎凭天性便通，新身份上身不出一个星期，我已能分辨地道的西班牙货同法国货；我十指戴满了戒指，两只表袋各揣一只怀表，手杖、小玩物、鼻烟壶形形色色，一件赛过一件的雅致。论花边同瓷器的天生意趣，我识得的人里没一个赶上我；相马的眼力，德国的犹太马贩也未必强过我；射击同健力诸技，我更是天下无对；我拼不出单词，德语法语却说得漂亮伶俐。我少说有12套衣裳：3套金线重绣，2套银线镶缘，1件石榴红天鹅绒皮氅，衬紫貂；1件法国灰，银缘滚边，衬青紫貂。我还有织锦晨袍。我学过吉他，法国轮唱曲唱得精妙绝伦。普天之下，哪里还寻得出一位比雷德蒙·德·巴利巴里更多才多艺的绅士？
当然，配得上我这份身份的种种奢华，离了赊头与银钱是办不来的：祖传的产业早教先人们挥霍尽了，经商又嫌鄙俗、回本慢、成败难卜，不是我们干的事，于是叔叔开了一座法罗赌庄。我们同一位佛罗伦萨人合伙——此公在欧洲各国宫廷无人不晓，便是亚历山德罗·皮皮伯爵，论赌技之精，举世罕见；可惜他后来露了馅，是个不折不扣的恶棍，我还查实，他那个伯爵衔纯属招摇撞骗。我叔叔身有残疾，前文表过；皮皮同一切骗子一样，胆小如鼠；可以说，全凭我剑术无双、说拔剑便拔剑，才撑住了我们这间字号的名头，镇住了多少输了钱想赖账的怯货。同什么人赌，我们都肯凭口头信用：也就是说，凡有荣誉、有门第之人。赢项我们从不催逼，人家拿期票来抵金路易，我们也从不推辞。只是期票到期不还的主儿——那可就自求多福吧！

===Original===

Redmond de Balibari was sure to wait upon him with his bill, and I promise you there were very few bad debts: on the contrary, gentlemen were grateful to us for our forbearance, and our character for honour stood unimpeached. In later times, a vulgar national prejudice has chosen to cast a slur upon the character of men of honour engaged in the profession of play; but I speak of the good old days in Europe, before the cowardice of the French aristocracy (in the shameful Revolution, which served them right) brought discredit and ruin upon our order. They cry fie now upon men engaged in play; but I should like to know how much more honourable *their* modes of livelihood are than ours. The broker of the Exchange who bulls and bears, and buys and sells, and dabbles with lying loans, and trades on State secrets, what is he but a gamester? The merchant who deals in teas and tallow, is he any better? His bales of dirty indigo are his dice, his cards come up every year instead of every ten minutes, and the sea is his green table. You call the profession of the law an honourable one, where a man will lie for any bidder; lie down poverty for the sake of a fee from wealth, lie down right because wrong is in his brief. You call a doctor an honourable man, a swindling quack, who does not believe in the nostrums which he prescribes, and takes your guinea for whispering in your ear that it is a fine morning; and yet, forsooth, a gallant man who sits him down before the baize and challenges all comers, his money against theirs, his fortune against theirs, is proscribed by your modern moral world. It is a conspiracy of the middle classes against gentlemen: it is only the shopkeeper cant which is to go down nowadays. I say that play was an institution of chivalry: it has been wrecked, along with other privileges of men of birth. When Seingalt engaged a man for six-and-thirty hours without leaving the table, do you think he showed no courage? How have we had the best blood, and the brightest eyes, too, of Europe throbbing round the table, as I and my uncle have held the cards and the bank against some terrible player, who was matching some thousands out of his millions against our all which was there on the baize!

===Chinese===

雷德蒙·德·巴利巴里必登门亲递账单，我担保，坏账寥寥可数：相反，绅士们倒感念我们的宽容，我们荣誉的名声无可訾议。到了近世，一种庸俗的时见偏要往吃赌这行的荣誉之士脸上泼污水；可我讲的是欧洲旧日的好时光，早在法国贵族那一场怯懦（在那场不名誉的革命里——对他们正合适）把我们的整个阶层拖进羞辱与覆灭之前。如今他们对赌钱之徒嗤之以鼻；我倒要请教，*他们*那几样谋生之道，又比我们的体面几分？交易所里翻多翻空、买进卖出、倒腾骗人的债券、拿国家机密做投机的掮客，不是赌棍是什么？贩茶叶牛油的买卖人，他又清高到哪里去？他那一捆捆脏靛青便是他的骰子，他的牌一年才开一回，不像我们的十分钟一回，大海便是他的绿呢赌台。你们称律师业为体面行业，可那行当里的人，谁出钱便替谁撒谎；为阔人一笔谢仪肯把穷人的理踩倒，因为状词里装着歪理便把公理踩倒。你们称医生为体面人——那摇铃卖药的骗子，自己也不信他开的那几味药，凑在你耳边说一句今儿天气真好，便收你一几尼；然而，一位堂堂君子往绿呢桌前一坐，向四方来者叫阵，以他的钱财对他们的钱财、以他的身家对他们的身家，竟教你们如今这个讲道德的世界给查禁了。这是中产阶级对绅士们的一场合谋：如今吃香的，不过是店主们的行话罢了。我要说，赌博本是骑士时代的一大建制：它同出身高贵者的其他种种特权一道，遭了灭顶。塞恩加尔特同人连局36个钟头不挪赌桌，你以为他没显出勇气？当我和叔叔执牌坐庄，迎战某个可怕的对家——他从百万家私里摘出几千，来搏我们押在绿呢上的全部家当——欧洲最高贵的血液、最明亮的眼睛，又何尝不曾在我们赌台四周激动地狂跳！

===Original===

when we engaged that daring Alexis Kossloffsky, and won seven thousand louis in a single coup, had we lost, we should have been beggars the next day; when *he* lost, he was only a village and a few hundred serfs in pawn the worse. When, at Toeplitz, the Duke of Courland brought fourteen lackeys, each with four bags of florins, and challenged our bank to play against the sealed bags, what did we ask? “Sir,” said we, “we have but eighty thousand florins in bank, or two hundred thousand at three months. If your Highness’s bags do not contain more than eighty thousand, we will meet you.” And we did, and after eleven hours’ play, in which our bank was at one time reduced to two hundred and three ducats, we won seventeen thousand florins of him. Is *this* not something like boldness? does *this* profession not require skill, and perseverance, and bravery? Four crowned heads looked on at the game, and an Imperial princess, when I turned up the ace of hearts and made Paroli burst into tears.
